## Preface / 前言

===Original===
The epic poem of *Gudrun* is one of the most important early literary works of the German race. It is attributed to the latter part of the twelfth or the beginning of the thirteenth century, and to a date a little subsequent to that of the *Nibelungenlied*. It was first brought to the notice of the modern world in the year 1817, the only original manuscript now known to exist having been discovered about that time in the castle Ambras in the Tyrol, among other manuscripts which had been collected by the Emperor Maximilian I (1493﻿–﻿1519). The manuscript is now in the Imperial Library at Vienna. It has been several times edited and printed in the original Middle High German, with critical annotations; various translations into modern German have also been published: but so far as I can learn, no complete metrical version in the English language has been made public.

===Chinese===
史诗《古德伦》（Gudrun）是日耳曼民族最重要的早期文学作品之一。其创作年代被断为十二世纪末或十三世纪初，略晚于《尼伯龙根之歌》（Nibelungenlied）。1817 年，这部史诗首次进入现代世界的视野：现今已知存世的唯一原始手稿，约于其时在蒂罗尔（Tyrol）的安布拉斯城（Ambras）被发现，与皇帝马克西米利安一世（Maximilian I，1493–1519）所搜集的其他手稿收存一处。该手稿今藏于维也纳的帝国图书馆。它已多次以中古高地德语原文校勘刊印，并附评注；现代德语的各种译本亦已出版；但据我所知，此前尚无完整的英文韵体译本公之于世。

===Original===
The name of the author is unknown; it is generally thought to have been constructed, in great measure, from earlier legends which had been repeated by wandering singers. According to the late Karl Bartsch, the distinguished critic and editor of Medieval German literature, the tale shows affinity to legends of the Scalds of Norway and Denmark, and to those of the Shetland Isles. Traces of resemblance are said to be found among the relics of Anglo-Saxon literature. The supposition that the poem was constructed from various early legends explains some of the marvellous incidents of the tale, and those chronological inconsistencies where the rude habits and ideas of earlier times are combined with the later knightly usages of the Middle Ages and with Christian belief.

===Chinese===
作者姓名已不可考；一般认为，这部史诗在很大程度上由游吟歌者世代传唱的早期传说缀合而成。据已故的中世纪德语文学杰出批评家兼校勘者卡尔·巴奇（Karl Bartsch）所言，这个故事与挪威、丹麦的斯卡尔徳（Scalds，即吟游诗人）传说以及设得兰群岛的传说均有亲缘；据说在盎格鲁-撒克逊文学的遗存中也能寻得相似的痕迹。认为此诗由多种早期传说缀合而成的假设，既解释了故事中若干神异的情节，也解释了那些年代上的错杂之处：较早时代的粗陋习尚与观念，竟与中世纪晚期的骑士风俗乃至基督教信仰交织并存。

===Original===
The scene of the poem is laid principally on the shores of the North Sea, and includes Ireland and Normandy, as well as Holland, Denmark, and Friesland. Very vague ideas of geography were, however, entertained by the poet. Some names of places are thought to be fabulous, and critics disagree with regard to the modern countries designated by other names used.

===Chinese===
故事的发生地主要设在北海之滨，涵盖爱尔兰与诺曼底，以及荷兰、丹麦和弗里西亚（Friesland）。不过，诗人怀有的地理观念相当模糊。有些地名被认为是凭空虚构，至于另一些地名所指为今天的哪些国家，批评家们则众说纷纭。

===Original===
The poem is founded upon the themes of love and war, and properly consists of three parts. The first portion, embracing four tales, relates the adventures of Hagen, the grandfather of Gudrun; the second part gives the story of the wooing and abduction of Hilda, his daughter, the mother of Gudrun. The proper story of Gudrun begins only with the ninth tale.

===Chinese===
这首诗以爱情与战争为主题，恰当地说由三个部分构成。第一部分共四个诗章，讲述古德伦的外祖父哈根（Hagen）的历险；第二部分叙述其女希尔达（Hilda）——即古德伦之母——被求娶与被劫夺的故事。古德伦本人的正传，到第九诗章方才开场。

===Original===
The narrative gives a vivid picture of the ideas, manners, and customs of the age of the author; of the dwellings, dress, and ornaments in use; of the weapons and warfare; of the ships and sea-life; and of the tournaments and court festivities. From it we see the nature of the intellectual amusement enjoyed by the northern nations, at that period of their mental development when literary entertainment in the modern sense was yet unknown, and its place was supplied by listening to the recitals of wandering bards.

===Chinese===
这首叙事诗生动描绘了作者那个时代的观念、礼仪与风俗；当时的居所、衣着与佩饰；兵器与战事；船舶与海上生活；以及比武大会与宫廷庆典。由此我们得以窥见北方民族当时所享的智性娱乐是何种性质——在他们心智发展的那个阶段，现代意义上的文学娱乐尚付阙如，倾听游吟歌者的吟诵便填补了这一空缺。

===Original===
Modern German critics agree in assigning a high literary value to the poem of *Gudrun*, and compare it not unfavorably with the *Nibelungenlied*. Bartsch, the critic above named, says: “The general impression which the poem gives is one of greater beauty, though not always of equal grandeur with that of the *Nibelungen*; it is a worthy companion-piece. The two are justly compared, as are the *Iliad* and the *Odyssey*. In the *Nibelungen* as in the *Iliad* the fate of a whole people is decided by the sword, and the ruling house, consisting of noble heroes, meets destruction before our eyes; but the conquerors do not fully rejoice in their success. The whole breathes a tragic spirit, even more than the Greek epic. ‘*Nach Freude Leid*’﻿—‘after joy comes sorrow’﻿—is the earnest tone throughout. *Gudrun*, like the *Odyssey*, closes more tenderly and in a spirit of reconciliation. Although pitiless fate has destroyed the happiness of those for whom the poet has awakened our sympathy, and we see a noble being suffer in the most shameful manner, yet we are sustained by hope, and a happy end compensates for woes endured. ‘*Aus Leid Freude*’﻿—‘sorrows end in joy’﻿—is the final conclusion.﻿ ﻿… All the characters are worked out in the most minute and careful manner, and are developed consistently.﻿ ﻿… The best traits of the German nature, fearless bravery, unfaltering fidelity, and unswerving integrity, are presented. The nobility of a feminine soul which, inspired by pure love, in the distress of a hard captivity, preserves its fidelity to its beloved, perhaps in no poem of the German middle ages is so strikingly shown as in the character of Gudrun.﻿ ﻿… The descriptions both of battle and scenery are masterly, and are painted with a few decisive strokes.”

===Chinese===
现代德国批评家一致给予《古德伦》以很高的文学评价，认为它与《尼伯龙根之歌》相比并不逊色。前文提到的批评家巴奇这样说道：「这首诗给人的总体印象是更为优美，虽然气魄未必总与《尼伯龙根》相当；它是一篇当之无愧的姊妹篇。这两部作品被相提并论是有道理的，正如《伊利亚特》与《奥德赛》并称一样。在《尼伯龙根》中，一如在《伊利亚特》中，整个民族的命运由刀剑裁决，由高贵英雄组成的统治王族在我们眼前走向毁灭；而征服者对自己的胜利也无法尽情欢庆。全篇洋溢着悲剧精神，甚至比希腊史诗更为浓重。『Nach Freude Leid』——『乐极生悲』——是贯穿全诗的沉郁基调。《古德伦》则如《奥德赛》一般，收束得较为温柔，满怀和解的精神。尽管无情的命运摧毁了诗人令我们寄予同情的那些人的幸福，我们目睹一个高贵的生命以最屈辱的方式受苦，但我们始终有希望支撑，圆满的结局补偿了所经受的种种伤痛。『Aus Leid Freude』——『苦尽甘来』——是最终的归结。……所有人物都以极细致、极审慎的笔法刻画而出，发展前后一贯。……日耳曼民族性中最优良的品性——无畏的勇武、坚定不移的忠贞、毫不动摇的正直——都得到了展现。一个女性的灵魂，因纯洁的爱而升华，在严酷囚禁的困厄中仍对所爱之人保持忠贞——在中世纪德语诗歌中，或许没有哪一部能像古德伦这一人物形象那样，把这种高贵展现得如此醒目。……无论战斗还是风景的描写皆属大师手笔，寥寥几笔果决的勾勒便宛然如绘。」

===Original===
The metrical form of *Gudrun* is similar to that of the *Nibelungen*. In both, the accentuation is determined by the logical sense, as in prose, and not always by the number of syllables, as in most modern verse. In both, the lines are metrically divided, giving three accents to each half-line of the stanza, except in the fourth line, in which *Gudrun* differs from the *Nibelungen* in having five accents instead of four on the concluding half-line.

===Chinese===
《古德伦》的格律形式与《尼伯龙根》相似。两者的重音都取决于逻辑语义，一如散文，而不像大多数现代诗那样取决于音节的数目。两者的诗行均按韵律划分为半行，诗节中每个半行各得三个重音；唯有第四行不同——《古德伦》的收尾半行带五个重音，而非《尼伯龙根》的四个。

===Original===
The translator has adhered to the original rhythm, and has endeavored in each stanza to convey strictly the ideas of the author, being careful not to introduce anything, in thought or simile, foreign to the poem, and, as far as the verse would permit, to give a verbal rendering.

===Chinese===
译者谨守原诗的节奏，力求在每一诗节中严格传达作者的意思，小心不引入任何在情思或譬喻上与原诗相违的成分，并在诗律容许的范围内尽量贴近字面译述。

===Original===
The translation has been made from Bartsch’s edition of the original Middle High German (Brockhaus, Leipzig, 1874). He, like the other editors, has supplied some omissions in the manuscript, an instance of which may be seen in the sixth line of the facsimile given. The drawings introduced are copied from medieval German printed books.

===Chinese===
本译本以巴奇校订的中古高地德语原文版本（布罗克豪斯出版社，莱比锡，1874 年）为底本。他与其他校勘者一样，补足了手稿中的一些脱漏，其例可见于所附影印件的第六行。书中所附的插图均摹绘自中世纪德国的印刷书籍。

===Original===
I wish to acknowledge my obligation to my brother for careful revision and for many important improvements throughout the translation.

===Chinese===
我谨向我的兄弟致谢，感谢他对译文通篇细心校订，并作出许多重要的改进。

===Original===
M. P. N.

===Chinese===
M. P. N.

===Original===
Boston, 1889.

===Chinese===
波士顿，1889 年。

## Names of Persons / 人名表

===Original===
- Ger﻿—an early king of Ireland, grandfather of Hagen.
- U-te﻿—wife of Ger.
- Sigeband﻿—son of Ger and U-te.
- U-te﻿—a Norwegian princess, wife of Sigeband.
- Hagen﻿—son of Sigeband and the second U-te, and grandfather of Gudrun.
- Hilda﻿—a princess of India, wife of Hagen.
- Hilda﻿—daughter of Hagen, wife of Hettel.

===Chinese===
- 格尔（Ger）——爱尔兰早期国王，哈根的祖父。
- 乌特（U-te）——格尔之妻。
- 西格班德（Sigeband）——格尔与乌特之子。
- 乌特（U-te）——挪威公主，西格班德之妻。
- 哈根（Hagen）——西格班德与第二位乌特之子，古德伦的外祖父。
- 希尔达（Hilda）——印度公主，哈根之妻。
- 希尔达（Hilda）——哈根之女，黑特尔之妻。

===Original===
- Hettel﻿—king of the Hegelings, husband of Hilda, and father of Gudrun and Ortwin.
- Gudrun﻿—daughter of Hettel and Hilda.
- Ortwin﻿—prince of Ortland, son of Hettel and Hilda.
- Wâ-te﻿—vassal of Hettel.
- Fru-te﻿—vassal of Hettel.
- Horant﻿—vassal of Hettel.
- Morunc﻿—vassal of Hettel.
- Irold﻿—vassal of Hettel.

===Chinese===
- 黑特尔（Hettel）——黑格林人之王，希尔达的丈夫，古德伦与奥特温之父。
- 古德伦（Gudrun）——黑特尔与希尔达之女。
- 奥特温（Ortwin）——奥特兰王子，黑特尔与希尔达之子。
- 瓦特（Wâ-te）——黑特尔之封臣。
- 弗鲁特（Fru-te）——黑特尔之封臣。
- 霍兰特（Horant）——黑特尔之封臣。
- 莫伦克（Morunc）——黑特尔之封臣。
- 伊罗尔德（Irold）——黑特尔之封臣。

===Original===
- Ludwig﻿—king of Normandy.
- Gerlind﻿—wife of Ludwig.
- Hartmut﻿—son of Ludwig and Gerlind, a suitor for Gudrun.
- Ortrun﻿—daughter of Ludwig and Gerlind.
- Siegfried﻿—king of Moorland, a suitor for Gudrun.
- Herwic﻿—king of Sealand, betrothed to Gudrun.

===Chinese===
- 路德维希（Ludwig）——诺曼底之王。
- 格尔林德（Gerlind）——路德维希之妻。
- 哈特穆特（Hartmut）——路德维希与格尔林德之子，古德伦的追求者。
- 奥特龙（Ortrun）——路德维希与格尔林德之女。
- 西格弗里德（Siegfried）——摩尔兰之王，古德伦的追求者。
- 赫尔维克（Herwic）——西兰之王，古德伦的未婚夫。

===Original===
- Hildeburg﻿—maiden companion of Gudrun.
- Hergart﻿—maiden companion of Gudrun.

===Chinese===
- 希尔德堡（Hildeburg）——古德伦的侍女伴。
- 赫加特（Hergart）——古德伦的侍女伴。

## Names of Places / 地名表

===Original===
- Abakie﻿—an imaginary Eastern land, subject to Siegfried.
- Abalie﻿—an Eastern land, noted for gems and cloths.
- Alzabie﻿—a fabulous Moorland city, the residence of Siegfried.
- Amilé﻿—an imaginary Eastern land, the home of mermaids.
- Araby﻿—a land whence came fine clothes and treasures.
- Ballian﻿—Ballyghan, Hagen’s chief city in Ireland.
- Daneland﻿—not the present Denmark, but, in the ninth century, the seat of the Danes in Friesland, near the mouth of the Scheldt.
- Dietmarsch﻿—a province subject to Hettel.
- Friesland﻿—subject to Hettel, and held in fief by Morunc and Irold.
- Galeis﻿—a land whose people are friendly to Herwic.
- Galicia﻿—Portugal, the home of Hildeburg.
- Garadie﻿—an indeterminate country, near Ireland.
- Givers﻿—a fabulous land, subject to Horant.
- Gulstred﻿—a place in the West.
- Hegeling﻿—the name of a people on the North Sea, in Holland, governed by Hettel.
- Holstein﻿—variously mentioned as subject to Fru-te, to Irolt, and to Ortwin.
- Icaria﻿—a fabulous land whose people are allies of Siegfried of Moorland.
- Ireland﻿—The situation seems sometimes to correspond with the modern Ireland, and sometimes to a part of Holland. There is a place in Texel, at the present day, named Eijerland.
- Iserland﻿—the home of one of Gudrun’s maiden companions.
- Kampalia﻿—a fabulous land noted for rich clothing.
- Kampatille﻿—Hettel’s castle, also called Matelan.
- Karadie﻿—a land belonging to Siegfried of Moorland.
- Kassian﻿—the chief city and castle of Normandy.
- Matelan﻿—see Kampatille.
- Moorland﻿—the kingdom of Siegfried; owing to the love of the marvelous in antiquity, regarded by the poet as the land of the Moors, but probably a low country near the North Sea.
- Nifland﻿—“the land of fogs,” on the lower Rhine, the home of the Nibelungen.
- Normandy or Ormanie﻿—may be the country now known as Normandy, or is perhaps a region near the mouth of the Scheldt, where the name Ormans-kapelle occurs in an ancient map.
- Ortland﻿—probably Jutland, under the rule of Ortwin.
- Salmé﻿—a fabulous country.
- Sealand﻿—Herwic’s kingdom, not the Danish Zealand, but probably the sea-lands of Friesland.
- Scotland﻿—spoken of as belonging to Norway.
- Sturmland﻿—subject to Wâ-te, adjoining Herwic’s kingdom.
- Waleis﻿—the western limit of Hettel’s kingdom, by some supposed to be Wales, but generally thought to be the country near the mouth of the river Waal in Holland.
- Wulpensand﻿—an island at the mouth of the Scheldt.

===Chinese===
- 阿巴基（Abakie）——虚构的东方之国，隶属西格弗里德（Siegfried）。
- 阿巴利（Abalie）——东方之国，以宝石与织物著称。
- 阿尔扎比（Alzabie）——传说中的摩尔兰城市，西格弗里德的居所。
- 阿米莱（Amilé）——虚构的东方之国，美人鱼的故乡。
- 阿拉比（Araby）——出产华服与珍宝的国度。
- 巴利安（Ballian）——即巴利根（Ballyghan），哈根（Hagen）在爱尔兰的都城。
- 丹麦兰（Daneland）——并非今之丹麦（Denmark），而是九世纪丹麦人聚居于弗里西亚（Friesland）的领地，靠近斯海尔德河（Scheldt）河口。
- 迪特马什（Dietmarsch）——隶属黑特尔（Hettel）的一省。
- 弗里西亚（Friesland）——隶属黑特尔，由莫伦克（Morunc）与伊罗尔德（Irold）领有封地。
- 加莱斯（Galeis）——其民亲善赫尔维克（Herwic）的国度。
- 加利西亚（Galicia）——即葡萄牙（Portugal），希尔德堡（Hildeburg）的故乡。
- 加拉迪（Garadie）——方位不明的国度，邻近爱尔兰。
- 吉弗斯（Givers）——传说中的国度，隶属霍兰特（Horant）。
- 古尔斯特雷德（Gulstred）——西方的一处地方。
- 黑格林（Hegeling）——北海（North Sea）一带一个族群之名，居于荷兰（Holland），受黑特尔统治。
- 荷尔斯泰因（Holstein）——书中或称隶属弗鲁特（Fru-te），或称隶属伊罗尔德（Irolt），或称隶属奥特温（Ortwin）。
- 伊卡利亚（Icaria）——传说中的国度，其民为摩尔兰西格弗里德的盟友。
- 爱尔兰（Ireland）——其方位时而似与今之爱尔兰相当，时而又似在荷兰的一部分境内。时至今日，特塞尔（Texel）仍有一地名为艾瑟兰（Eijerland）。
- 伊瑟兰（Iserland）——古德伦（Gudrun）一位侍女伴的故乡。
- 坎帕利亚（Kampalia）——传说中的国度，以华服著称。
- 坎帕蒂勒（Kampatille）——黑特尔的城堡，又名马特兰。
- 卡拉迪（Karadie）——隶属摩尔兰西格弗里德的国度。
- 卡西安（Kassian）——诺曼底的都城与城堡。
- 马特兰（Matelan）——见坎帕蒂勒。
- 摩尔兰（Moorland）——西格弗里德的王国；因古人嗜好奇异之谈，诗人视之为摩尔人（Moors）的国度，但多半实为北海附近的一片低地。
- 尼夫兰（Nifland）——「雾之国」，位于莱茵河（Rhine）下游，尼伯龙根人（Nibelungen）的家园。
- 诺曼底（Normandy）或奥尔马尼（Ormanie）——可能即今所称的诺曼底，也可能是斯海尔德河口一带的地区，古地图上该处标有「奥尔曼斯卡佩勒」（Ormans-kapelle）之名。
- 奥特兰（Ortland）——或即日德兰（Jutland），由奥特温统治。
- 萨尔梅（Salmé）——传说中的国度。
- 西兰（Sealand）——赫尔维克的王国，并非丹麦的西兰岛（Zealand），而多半是弗里西亚的海滨之地。
- 苏格兰（Scotland）——书中称其为挪威（Norway）的属地。
- 施图尔姆兰（Sturmland）——隶属瓦特（Wâ-te），与赫尔维克的王国相接。
- 瓦莱斯（Waleis）——黑特尔王国的西界，有人以为是威尔士（Wales），一般认为则是荷兰瓦尔河（Waal）河口一带之地。
- 武尔彭沙洲（Wulpensand）——斯海尔德河口的一座岛屿。

## Tale I How Hagen Was Carried Off by the Griffin / 第一诗章 哈根如何被狮鹫掳走

===Original===
Sigeband, king of Ireland, after the death of his father, marries a princess of Norway, who bears him a son, Hagen. During the celebration of a court festival, Hagen, now seven years old, while outside the castle in the care of his attendants, is caught up by a griffin, and borne to his nest in a distant land.

===Chinese===
爱尔兰国王西格班德（Sigeband）在父王死后，迎娶了一位挪威公主；公主为他生下一子哈根（Hagen）。一次宫廷庆典举行之际，年方七岁的哈根在城堡外由侍从照看，被一只狮鹫攫去，带往遥远之国它的巢中。

===Original===
In olden days in Ireland
 a king to greatness came
 Who bore the name of Sigeband;
 Ger was his father’s name.

===Chinese===
昔日在爱尔兰，
一位国王勃兴而昌，
他的名号是西格班德；
其父名叫格尔（Ger）。

===Original===
 Queen U-te was his mother;
 she of a king was daughter;
 High was her worth and goodness,
 and well her love beseemed the lord who sought her.

===Chinese===
他的母亲是乌特（U-te）王后，
她本是一位国王之女；
她品性高贵仁善，
她的爱情正配那位前来求娶的君主。

===Original===
The sway of Ger was mighty,
 as unto all is known;
 He many lands and castles
 and lordships seven did own:

===Chinese===
格尔的权势何等煊赫，
世人无不知晓；
他坐拥众多邦国城堡，
还领有七方领地：

===Original===
 Four thousand knights or over
 he thence was often leading,
 And wealth, and name yet greater,
 he daily won, with those who did his bidding.

===Chinese===
四千骑士犹不止，
他常统领着他们，
愈发雄厚的财富与名声，
他靠听命于他的人们日日赢得。

===Original===
Now the youthful Sigeband
 to his father’s court must go,
 That he might there be learning
 all he had need to know﻿—

===Chinese===
如今年少的西格班德
须赴父亲的宫廷，
好在那里学习
一切他该通晓的本事——

===Original===
 To bear the spear in riding,
 to thrust it, and to shield him,
 That when he met the foeman,
 the better fame thereby the fight would yield him.

===Chinese===
骑乘时执着长枪，
刺击，并护住自身，
待到与仇敌相逢，
好让战斗为他赢得更美的声名。

===Original===
That age he now was reaching
 when he the sword might bear;
 Of all that a knight befitteth
 he learned a goodly share.

===Chinese===
他已长到
可以佩剑的年纪；
凡与骑士相称的本领，
他都学得了可观的一份。

===Original===
 This from kin and vassals
 praise unmeasured brought him;
 For this he still was striving,
 and of the toil it cost he ne’er bethought him.

===Chinese===
这为他自亲族与封臣处
赢得了无量的赞誉；
他为此仍孜孜以求，
从不把其中的辛劳放在心上。

===Original===
A few short days thereafter
 death came among them all,
 As even to men the greatest
 sadly doth befall.

===Chinese===
此后不过短短数日，
死神便降临他们中间，
正如纵是最伟大的人物，
也难逃悲伤的劫数。

===Original===
 In every land and kingdom
 the truth of this we’re meeting,
 And we, with heavy sorrow,
 such news ourselves must every day be waiting.

===Chinese===
在每一片土地与王国，
我们都遇见这真理，
而我们怀着沉重的悲哀，
每日只得坐候这样的消息。

===Original===
Sigeband’s mother, U-te,
 the widow’s seat must take;
 Her son, so high and worthy,
 left all things for her sake.

===Chinese===
西格班德之母乌特，
只得安坐寡妇之位；
她的儿子如此高贵贤良，
为她的缘故抛下了一切。

===Original===
 No whit he cared for wedlock,
 and had no heart for wooing;
 Many a queenly lady
 at this was sad, young Sigeband’s sorrow ruing.

===Chinese===
他全然无心婚娶，
也无意志于求爱；
多少王后般尊贵的淑女
为此伤怀，哀怜年轻的西格班德的愁绪。

===Original===
A worthy wife to find him
 his mother him besought;
 So might he and his kingdom
 to greater name be brought;

===Chinese===
为他觅一位贤德的妻子，
母亲恳切相劝；
如此他同他的王国
便能赢得更隆的名望；

===Original===
 And he with all his kindred,
 after their bitter sorrow
 For the death of the king, his father,
 might for themselves no little gladness borrow.

===Chinese===
他和他的全体亲族，
在为先王——他的父亲——
之死深哀之后，
也可为自己求得不少欢愉。

===Original===
The teaching of his mother
 he heard in kindly mood,
 And began at once to follow,
 as that of a friend one should.

===Chinese===
母亲的一番教诲，
他欣然听纳，
随即开始遵行，
一如对待挚友的忠言。

===Original===
 The best of high-born maidens,
 ’mong those in Norway dwelling,
 He bade his men to sue for:
 to help in this he found his kinsmen willing.

===Chinese===
在居于挪威的名门少女之中，
凡出身最高贵的，
他都遣人前去求娶：
在此事上，他也得到亲族乐意相助。

===Original===
She soon to him was wedded,
 as hath of old been said.
 With her, among her followers,
 came many a lovely maid,

===Chinese===
她不久便与他完婚，
一如古来所传。
随她同来的随员之中，
有许多可爱的少女，

===Original===
 And, from over Scotland’s border,
 seven hundred warriors fully;
 They came with her right gladly,
 when the worth of the king was known to them more truly.

===Chinese===
还有越过苏格兰边界的
足七百名武士；
他们欣然随她前来，
那时他们对国王的贤德已识得更真。

===Original===
Proudly their way they wended,
 as beseemed the maiden’s birth;
 With all the care they led her
 befitting his kingly worth;

===Chinese===
他们一路行来何等气派，
恰配少女的高贵出身；
他们护送她何等周到，
恰合他的君王之尊；

===Original===
 Hidden were the roadways
 by gazers without number,
 Who hasted to behold her;
 for three miles and a half the throngs the ways did cumber.

===Chinese===
条条道路
都被数不清的观者遮满，
他们争先赶来一睹她的容颜；
三英里半的路段，人群壅塞了道路。

===Original===
Where’er along the roadside
 the path with green was spread,
 Flowers and grass were trampled,
 by crowds, with heavy tread.

===Chinese===
沿路四处，
凡青翠铺展的小径，
花草尽被人群
沉重的脚步践踏。

===Original===
 It fell upon that season
 when the leaves are springing,
 And in every copse and thicket
 all the birds their best of songs are singing.

===Chinese===
时逢草木
萌叶抽新的季节，
每一处丛林灌莽，
群鸟都唱着最好的歌。

===Original===
Of simple folk and merry
 there rode with her enough;
 While many loaded horses
 bore much costly stuff,

===Chinese===
淳朴而快活的人们，
随她骑行的不在少数；
许多满载的马匹
驮着大宗贵重的财物，

===Original===
 Brought there from her birthland
 by followers of the maiden;
 They came with her by thousands,
 with gold as well as clothing heavy-laden.

===Chinese===
都是少女的随从们
从她的故土运来；
他们成千上万随她而来，
黄金与衣装满载沉沉。

===Original===
On the shore of two wide marches,
 the dwellers by the sea,
 As they saw the west wind waft her,
 gave her welcome free;

===Chinese===
在两片辽阔边地的海滨，
海畔的居民们，
眼见西风送她前来，
便向她致以由衷的欢迎；

===Original===
 They found a seemly lodging
 for the lovely, well-born lady,
 And brought her all things needful,
 by the youthful king, before, for her made ready.

===Chinese===
他们为这位可爱的高贵淑女
觅得体面的居所，
为她送来一切必需之物，
皆是年轻国王先前为她备办的。

===Original===
The fair young maid they welcomed
 with knightly tournament;
 Not soon their games they ended,
 when on the spear-fight bent.

===Chinese===
人们以骑士的比武大会
迎接美丽的少女；
一心较枪争先，
他们的游戏便迟迟不散。

===Original===
 To the land of Ger his father
 they bore her to be wedded;
 She there was loved and mighty,
 and men to sound her name she never needed.

===Chinese===
他们送她前往
其父格尔的国土完婚；
她在彼处受敬爱而有威权，
从不需要人们去传扬她的名声。

===Original===
All, as they were able,
 waited on the maid;
 The gaudy cloth for her saddle
 down to the grass was spread;

===Chinese===
人人各尽其力，
侍奉这位少女；
她鞍上华美的锦褥
一直垂铺到草地；

===Original===
 The horses’ hoofs were hidden
 by the housing, heavy drooping.
 Aha! In mood how gleeful
 was Ireland’s lord, once more a blessing hoping!

===Chinese===
连马蹄也被
沉沉垂坠的马帔遮住。
啊哈！爱尔兰君王的心情
何等欢畅，只盼福分重临！

===Original===
When now the time was fitting
 that he the maid should kiss,
 All crowded thick about him,
 in haste to see their bliss.

===Chinese===
到了合宜的时辰，
他该亲吻那少女，
众人稠密地簇拥上来，
急欲亲见他们的幸福。

===Original===
 The bosses of their bucklers
 were now heard loudly clashing,
 Struck with blows together;
 each strove to shun the throngs, in uproar crashing.

===Chinese===
圆盾上的盾钉
此刻铿然交鸣，
被彼此敲击作响；
人人都想避开那喧嚣鼎沸的人群。

===Original===
Now with the dawn of morning,
 they sent out, far and wide,
 To give to all the tidings
 of the coming of the bride,

===Chinese===
清晨破晓时分，
信使四出，广布远近，
向众人传报
新娘到来的喜讯，

===Original===
 And that, with their master,
 they erelong would crown her.
 His queen she was thereafter,
 and well she earned from him the honor shown her.

===Chinese===
并说她不久便要同他们君主
一同加冕为后。
此后她便是他的王后，
他所赐的尊荣，她受之无愧。

===Original===
It was not deemed becoming
 that he his love should plight,
 Since she by birth was queenly,
 and he not yet a knight:

===Chinese===
世人认为不合体统：
他不该先行缔结婚约，
因她生来便具王后之贵，
而他尚未受封为骑士：

===Original===
 He first, before his lieges,
 must the crown be wearing;
 To this his kinsmen helped him,
 and later of his worth were all men hearing.

===Chinese===
他须先当着众封臣
把王冠戴上；
众亲族助他成礼，
后来世人也听闻了他的贤能。

===Original===
He, with knights five hundred,
 then was dubbed with the sword;
 Whatever they could wish for
 was given them at his word﻿—

===Chinese===
他连同五百名骑士，
一同受剑册封；
他们心中想要的一切，
都凭他一句话赏给——

===Original===
 Both shields, and, for their wearing,
 every kind of clothing.
 The youthful king so dauntless,
 thro’ life, of fame and honor wanted nothing.

===Chinese===
既有盾牌，也有
供他们穿戴的各色衣装。
这位年轻国王如此无畏，
一生于名望与荣耀无所欠缺。

===Original===
For many a day thereafter
 his sway did Ireland bless,
 And never did his greatness
 at any time grow less.

===Chinese===
此后经年累月，
他的治世福泽爱尔兰，
他的伟业
从未有一时衰减。

===Original===
 To all he freely listened;
 the poor man’s wrongs he righted;
 Widely known was his goodness;
 no truer knight than he his word e’er plighted.

===Chinese===
他乐于倾听众人；
为穷人昭雪冤屈；
他的仁善广为人知；
再无骑士比他更恪守信约。

===Original===
His boundless acres yielded
 a full and ready gain;
 His wife was known for wisdom,
 and worthy to be his queen.

===Chinese===
他无边的田亩
出产丰饶而及时；
他的妻子以智慧见称，
堪配王后之位。

===Original===
 To hold her as their mistress
 full thirty lords it booted;
 As long as the sway she wielded,
 her hand to each his lands and home allotted.

===Chinese===
三十位领主
都因奉她为主母而受益；
只在她执掌权柄期间，
她亲手为每人分派领地与家园。

===Original===
She bore unto her husband,
 within the next three years,
 A child to see most comely;
 (such is the tale one hears.)

===Chinese===
此后三年之内，
她为丈夫生下一子，
一个眉目至美的孩子；
（人们听传的便是如此。）

===Original===
 When later he was christened,
 and they were told to name him,
 They gave the name of Hagen;
 and never since, the tale of his life doth shame him.

===Chinese===
后来他受洗之时，
人们要为他命名，
他们给他取名哈根；
此后他一生的传说从未使他蒙羞。

===Original===
He had most careful breeding,
 and kindly was he nursed;
 Should he be like his fathers,
 he would of knights be first.

===Chinese===
他受着最精心的教习，
被温慈地哺育；
倘若他肖似他的先辈，
他将成为骑士之冠。

===Original===
 Watched over by wise women,
 and by maidens of early age,
 His father and fond mother
 found in his face their glad eyes’ pasturage.

===Chinese===
有睿智的妇人们照看他，
还有年少的女郎们，
他的父亲与慈母
欢悦的双眼在他脸上觅得牧场。

===Original===
When now the boy, well fostered,
 to his seventh year was bred,
 ’Twas seen that he by warriors
 by the hand was often led.

===Chinese===
这男孩养育得宜，
已长到七岁之年，
人们常见武士们
牵着他的手行走。

===Original===
 He was happy in men’s teaching,
 but was with women wearied;
 All this he knew no longer;
 for, torn from them, he far away was carried.

===Chinese===
他乐于受男人们教习，
却厌倦与妇人们为伴；
这一切他不久便不再知晓；
因为他被从她们身边夺走，掳往远方。

===Original===
Whene’er to him it happened
 weapons at court to see,
 He understood them readily,
 and their wearer longed to be;

===Chinese===
每逢他在宫廷里
偶然得见兵器，
他一眼便能识得，
渴望成为佩带它们的人；

===Original===
 The helmet and ringed armor
 would he have put on gladly:
 Alas! not long he saw them,
 and all his hopes of fighting ended sadly.

===Chinese===
头盔与环甲，
他多想欣然穿戴：
唉！他见它们的日子何其短促，
他征战的万般希望都惨然落空。

===Original===
While the kingly Sigeband,
 beneath a cedar-tree,
 One day on the turf was seated,
 the queen said earnestly:

===Chinese===
一日，尊贵的西格班德
在雪松之下，
坐在草茵之上，
王后恳切说道：

===Original===
 “Although good name and riches
 we share with one another,
 At one thing yet I wonder,
 and this from you I dare to hide no further.”

===Chinese===
「虽说美名与财富
你我彼此共享，
有一事我却暗自疑惑，
再不敢向你隐瞒。」

===Original===
He asked of her: “What is it?”
 Then said his helpmeet kind:
 “It me doth sorely worry
 in body and in mind,

===Chinese===
他问她：「是什么事？」
于是他善良的伴侣说道：
「这事苦苦折磨我，
使我身心俱疲，

===Original===
 And my heart, alas! is heavy;
 to my wish you give no heeding,
 To see you ’midst your vassals,
 my beaming eyes with pride upon you feeding.”

===Chinese===
我的心，唉，何等沉重；
你却全不理会我的心愿——
想看你置身于封臣之间，
让我明亮的眼睛以你为傲。」

===Original===
The king to her thus answered:
 “How should it ever be
 That you have had such longing
 me with my knights to see?

===Chinese===
国王这样回答她：
「这从何说起——
你竟怀有这样的渴望，
想看我与骑士们相聚？

===Original===
 I will strive thy will to follow,
 of this think not so sadly;
 Ever to meet thy wishes,
 both care and toil will I give myself most gladly.”

===Chinese===
我必努力顺从你的心意，
莫为此事这般忧戚；
为时时遂你的愿望，
操劳与辛苦我都极甘愿承当。」

===Original===
She said: “No man is living
 who owns such wealth, I trow,
 Who has so many castles
 or lands so wide as thou,

===Chinese===
她说：「据我所信，世上再无一人
拥有这般财富，
拥有这么多城堡，
和如你这般辽阔的国土，

===Original===
 With silver and gems so costly,
 and gold so heavy weighing;
 For this are our ways too lowly,
 and nought there is in life to me worth saying.

===Chinese===
有如此贵重的白银与宝石，
和称起来沉重的黄金；
正因如此，我们的起居太过寒素，
生活里再无一事值得我挂齿。」

===Original===
“When erst I was a maiden,
 and on Scotland’s soil drew breath,
 (Chide not, my lord, thy helpmeet,
 but list to what she saith,)

===Chinese===
「想当年我还是少女，
在苏格兰的土地上呼吸，
（我的君王，莫怪你的伴侣，
且听她要说的话，）

===Original===
 I there was daily seeing
 the liegemen of my father
 For highest prizes striving;
 but here such games we never see together.

===Chinese===
我在那里日日得见
我父亲的封臣们
争夺最高的奖赏；
可在这里，这样的竞技我们从未同观。」

===Original===
“A king so rich and mighty,
 as you in name have been,
 Before his followers often
 should let himself be seen;

===Chinese===
「一位如你这般
名实相副的富贵君王，
本该常在随从们眼前
现身露面；

===Original===
 He oft should ride in tilting
 with other champions knightly,
 That both himself and his kingdom
 should seem more fair, and hold their rank more fitly.

===Chinese===
他该常与其他骑士豪杰
驰马比武，
这样他本人连同他的王国
才更见光彩，名位也更相称。」

===Original===
“It shows, in a lord so noble,
 a most unworthy mind,
 When he has heaped together
 riches of every kind,

===Chinese===
「一位如此高贵的领主，
当他聚敛了
形形色色的财富，
却显出最不足取的心肠，

===Original===
 If he with his faithful warriors
 to share them is unwilling:
 When men in the storm of warfare
 deep wounds have had, how else can they find healing?”

===Chinese===
连与忠诚的武士们分享
都心有不甘：
武士们在战争的风暴里
身受重创，此外更从何处得愈？」

===Original===
Then said to her King Sigeband:
 “Lady, you mock at me;
 In all these warlike pastimes
 I will most earnest be;

===Chinese===
于是西格班德国王对她说：
「夫人，你是在打趣我；
这一切尚武的游乐，
我必认真以赴；

===Original===
 And for the strife so worthy
 my wish shall never waver:
 No man shall find it easy
 the ways of well-born kings to teach me ever.”

===Chinese===
为这值得从事的争衡，
我的心愿绝不动摇：
任谁也休想轻易把
高门君王之道来教我。」

===Original===
She said: “You now for warriors
 must send throughout the land;
 Stores of wealth and clothing
 must be given with open hand.

===Chinese===
她说：「如今你须遣使
向全国征召武士；
财货与衣装
须慷慨出手分发。

===Original===
 I too will send out heralds
 my kinsmen all to rally,
 And to show them my good wishes;
 we then shall find our life to pass more gaily.”

===Chinese===
我也将派出传令官，
召集我全部亲族，
并向他们致我的美意；
那时我们将见日子过得愈发欢畅。」

===Original===
At this the king of Ireland
 unto his wife thus said:
 “I yield to you most willingly,
 for men are often led

===Chinese===
闻此，爱尔兰的国王
对妻子这般说道：
「我心甘情愿依从你，
因为男人们常常被

===Original===
 By the wishes of fair women
 great feastings to make ready;
 I therefore now will gather
 my brave and hardy kinsmen, and those too of my lady.”

===Chinese===
美貌女子的心愿
引去张罗盛大的宴庆；
所以我现在就要召集
我勇悍的亲族，连同你娘家的亲族。」

===Original===
To him the queen then answered:
 “Sorrow no more I wear;
 Five hundred women’s garments
 I will give, to each her share;

===Chinese===
王后随即答他：
「我心头悲伤已消；
五百件妇人衣裙
我愿分赠，各得其份；

===Original===
 To four and sixty maidens
 gay clothes to give I’m willing.”
 Then the king did tell her
 high times he soon would hold, his word fulfilling.

===Chinese===
还有六十四位少女，
我也乐意赠予鲜丽的衣裳。」
于是国王告诉她，
他不久便要大张盛会，践履诺言。

===Original===
The sports were then bespoken:
 he bade his men to send,
 In eighteen days or sooner,
 to liegeman and to friend,

===Chinese===
竞技之事就此议定：
他命属下前去传信，
限十八日之内或更早，
送达各位封臣与友朋，

===Original===
 To say to all in Ireland,
 who would in his games be riding,
 That, after summer was ended,
 they should spend the winter, with him abiding.

===Chinese===
告知全爱尔兰凡愿
驰骋于他竞技的人：
夏日结束之后，
都来与他一同过冬。

===Original===
He bade his men make benches,
 so our tale doth run,
 And for these, from out the wilderness,
 timber must be drawn;

===Chinese===
他命属下打造长凳，
我们的故事如此传说，
为此还须从莽莽荒野
运回木材；

===Original===
 For sixty thousand warriors
 seats must they make ready.
 His henchmen and deft stewards,
 to do this work for the king, were skilled and speedy.

===Chinese===
为六万名武士，
他们要备妥座席。
他的近侍与干练的司务，
为王操办此事，既娴熟又迅捷。

===Original===
Thither men then hastened
 on many a winding way;
 All were kindly cared for
 throughout their lengthened stay.

===Chinese===
人们于是循着
一条条迂回的道路奔赴那里；
在漫长的盘桓期间，
人人皆受周到的照拂。

===Original===
 Now from Ireland’s kingdom,
 as the king had bidden,
 Full six and eighty thousand
 of warriors strong there to his court had ridden.

===Chinese===
于是从爱尔兰王国，
如国王所吩咐，
足八万六千名
强健的武士驰抵他的宫廷。

===Original===
From the store-rooms of the castle
 clothing now was borne﻿—
 All the gear they wished for,
 and all that could be worn.

===Chinese===
这时从城堡的库房里
搬出了衣装——
既有他们想要的一切器械，
也有一切可以穿用的衣物。

===Original===
 Shields were also given,
 and steeds of Irish breeding;
 The proud and queenly lady
 bedecked her guests with all they could be needing.

===Chinese===
盾牌也一并赏发，
还有爱尔兰所产的骏马；
那位高傲而端庄的王后
为宾客们置办了所需的一切。

===Original===
She gave to a thousand women
 costly clothes enow,
 And likewise to fair maidens
 what one to youth should allow﻿—

===Chinese===
她赠给一千名妇人
充足的华服，
也赠给美丽的少女们
青春年华所宜有的物件——

===Original===
 Broidered bands and jewels,
 and silk that glistened brightly;
 The many lovely ladies,
 together standing there, were fair and sightly.

===Chinese===
绣花的束带与珠宝，
还有莹莹闪耀的丝绸；
众多可爱的淑女
聚立一处，明艳照人。

===Original===
To every one who wished it
 were given clothes well-made.
 Horses were there seen prancing,
 by the hand of foot-boys led;

===Chinese===
凡属心所愿的人
都得到剪裁合体的衣装。
但见骏马昂首腾跃，
由马童们牵行，

===Original===
 These light shields did carry,
 and their spears were seizing.
 U-te, the queenly mother,
 was gladly seen, as she on the leads sat gazing.

===Chinese===
马童们捧着轻盾，
又将长枪握起。
乌特，尊贵的国母，
欣然可见，正坐在屋顶平台上眺望。

===Original===
The guests by the king were bidden
 freely in tilts to meet;
 The glitter of their helmets
 grew dim in the dust and heat.

===Chinese===
国王请宾客们
自由配对比枪；
头盔上的辉光
在尘土与燠热里黯淡。

===Original===
 The ladies, held in honor,
 near by were also seated,
 Where they the deeds of the warriors
 saw full well, and with words of wonder greeted.

===Chinese===
受尊崇的淑女们
也安坐在近旁，
武士们的身手她们看得真切，
以惊叹之辞相称赏。

===Original===
As oft before has happened,
 the show had lasted long;
 The king was not unwilling
 to be looked on by the throng.

===Chinese===
正如往日常有的情形，
这场表演延续了许久；
国王也并不介意
受众人瞻仰。

===Original===
 This, meanwhile, to his lady
 happiness was giving,
 As she, amidst her women,
 sat on the roof, and saw their earnest striving.

===Chinese===
与此同时，这给他的夫人
带来了欢愉，
她坐在自家女眷中间的屋顶上，
望着他们奋力的争锋。

===Original===
When now her lord had ridden,
 as doth beseem a king,
 He thought to end their onsets;
 some rest to them to bring

===Chinese===
当她的夫君
以君王之仪驰骋已毕，
他便想结束众人的进击；
让他们得些歇息，

===Original===
 He deemed not unbecoming;
 to stop the games he bade them.
 And then before the ladies,
 after their skill thus shown, he proudly led them.

===Chinese===
他觉得这并无不妥；
便命他们停了竞技。
随后，在众人献过身手之后，
他骄傲地领着他们来到淑女们面前。

===Original===
U-te, the high-born lady,
 began her friends to greet,
 With those from far-off kingdoms;
 them as guests to meet

===Chinese===
乌特，出身高贵的夫人，
开始向她的亲友致意，
也招呼远方王国来的宾客；
迎接他们前来作客，

===Original===
 The queen was truly willing;
 on them her glad eyes rested.
 The gifts of Lady U-te
 were not on scornful friends that evening wasted.

===Chinese===
王后诚心乐意；
她欢悦的目光停驻在他们身上。
乌特夫人的礼物
那晚未曾虚掷于轻慢之客。

===Original===
Knights and lovely ladies
 together there were seen.
 The goodwill of the master
 to all well-known had been;

===Chinese===
骑士与可爱的淑女们
在那里相聚同堂。
主人的善意
早已为众人所周知；

===Original===
 In all their games and tilting,
 his kindness was not hidden.
 Once more the guests, that evening,
 to ride in warlike strife by him were bidden.

===Chinese===
在他们一切的竞技比枪之中，
他的仁厚毫不掩饰。
当晚，宾客们
又一次蒙他相邀，驰马鏖战。

===Original===
Their games and sports had lasted
 until nine days were gone;
 They, as knights befitteth,
 their skill to the king had shown.

===Chinese===
他们的游戏与竞技
一直延续到九天期满；
他们如骑士所当为，
向国王展示了身手。

===Original===
 By the many wandering players
 the show was liked the better,
 And they plied their work more briskly,
 and hoped that their reward would be the greater.

===Chinese===
众多四处游走的艺人
对这场盛会愈发称心，
他们卖艺越发起劲，
只盼赏赐也更见丰厚。

===Original===
Sackbuts loud and trumpets
 there might all men hear;
 Fluting too and harping
 fell upon the ear.

===Chinese===
嘹亮的萨克布号与号管，
在那里人人皆得听闻；
还有笛韵与竖琴之声，
声声传入耳中。

===Original===
 Some on the rote were playing,
 others in song were vying;
 They, by their jigs and fifing,
 soon would better clothes for themselves be buying.

===Chinese===
有人弹起罗特琴，
有人竞展歌喉；
他们凭舞曲与笛乐，
不久便能为自己购置更好的衣裳。

===Original===
On the tenth morn it happened,
 (now hark to my sorry tale,)
 That, after all their pastimes,
 there rose a bitter wail.

===Chinese===
到了第十天清晨，
（且听我这悲伤的故事，）
在一切欢娱过后，
腾起了一片凄厉的哀号。

===Original===
 About these days so merry
 new tales were told on the morrow;
 And though they now were mirthful,
 they came to know deep gloom and heavy sorrow.

===Chinese===
关于这些欢乐的日子，
次日又生出了新的传闻；
虽然他们此刻还满怀欢欣，
却即将深陷阴郁与沉重的哀伤。

===Original===
When the guests were seated
 beside their kingly host,
 There came to them a player,
 and proudly made his boast

===Chinese===
当宾客们
在君王主人身畔落座，
来了一个艺人，
骄傲地自夸

===Original===
 That he, before all others,
 (who should indeed believe him?)
 Was far more skilled in playing,
 and even the greatest lords their ear must give him.

===Chinese===
说他胜过所有人
（谁还真会信他？），
技艺远为高超，
连最尊贵的领主也须倾耳听他。

===Original===
Outside, a lovely maiden
 was leading by the hand
 The little son of Sigeband
 who swayed the Irish land;

===Chinese===
外面，一位美丽的少女
正手牵着手，
领着西格班德的幼子——
那统治爱尔兰的君王之子；

===Original===
 With him were likewise women
 who to the boy gave heeding,
 And friendly kinsmen also,
 who carefully taught the child, and oversaw his breeding.

===Chinese===
随侍孩子的还有妇人们，
在旁悉心照看，
还有友善的亲族，
细心教导孩童，督察他的教养。

===Original===
Within the great king’s palace
 was heard a din and shout;
 All were there heard laughing,
 the roomy walls throughout.

===Chinese===
在大国王的宫殿里，
响起了喧闹与呼喝；
处处都听得见欢笑，
传遍宽敞的殿墙内外。

===Original===
 The guardians of young Hagen
 crowded up too nearly,
 And thus lost sight of the maiden,
 together with the child they loved so dearly.

===Chinese===
小哈根的看护者们
挤凑得太近，
就这样望丢了那少女，
连同他们挚爱的孩子。

===Original===
The evil luck of their master
 to him that day drew near,
 And brought to him and U-te
 sudden woe and fear.

===Chinese===
他们主人的厄运
那一天向他逼近，
给他和乌特
带来骤然的灾殃与惊惶。

===Original===
 Sent by the wicked devil,
 from afar his herald hasted
 To them in their happy kingdom;
 they were by this with sorrow sorely wasted.

===Chinese===
那是恶毒的魔鬼差遣的信使，
自远方疾疾赶来，
闯入他们幸福的王国；
他们被此事摧折，憔悴于深哀。

===Original===
It was a strong, wild griffin
 had quickly thither flown;
 From the little boy of Sigeband,
 who ever care had known,

===Chinese===
那是一只强壮狂野的狮鹫，
倏然飞临彼处；
从西格班德的幼子——
那从未识得忧患的孩子——

===Original===
 Came ill luck to his father,
 who soon of this was tasting.
 His son, so well-belovèd,
 to him was lost, with the mighty bird far hasting.

===Chinese===
厄运降到他父亲身上，
他不久便尝到了苦味。
他那样深爱的儿子，
就此失去，随那巨鸟疾飞远去。

===Original===
A shadow now came o’er them,
 from wings that bore him fleet,
 As if a cloud had risen;
 great strength had the bird, I weet.

===Chinese===
一道阴影此时罩住他们，
来自疾掠的劲翼，
仿佛涌起了一片乌云；
那鸟力大无穷，我深然知晓。

===Original===
 The guests, in pastime busy,
 no thought to this had given,
 And the maid, with the child she was leading,
 was standing now alone, unheeded even.

===Chinese===
宾客们正耽于游乐，
对此毫未留意，
那少女，还牵着她的孩子，
此刻独自伫立，无人理睬。

===Original===
Beneath the weight of the griffin
 forest trees broke down;
 And now the trusty maiden
 looked where the bird had flown;

===Chinese===
在狮鹫的重压之下，
林木纷纷摧折；
这时那忠实的少女
才望向巨鸟飞去的方向；

===Original===
 Then she herself sought shelter,
 and left the child forsaken.
 Hearing a tale so startling,
 one truly might the whole for a wonder reckon.

===Chinese===
随后她自己寻处躲避，
抛下被弃的孩子。
听到这样骇人的故事，
人们真会把它当作奇事一桩。

===Original===
The griffin soon alighted,
 and in his claws he held
 The little child, gripped tightly,
 while with fear it quailed.

===Chinese===
狮鹫旋即落地，
利爪紧紧攥着
那幼小的孩子，
孩子吓得瑟瑟发抖。

===Original===
 His ghastly mood and anger
 the bird was harshly showing;
 This must knights and kinsmen
 long bewail, with sorrow ever growing.

===Chinese===
那鸟凶戾的心性与暴怒
狰狞地显露出来；
骑士与亲族们必将
为此长久哀恸，忧伤与日俱增。

===Original===
The boy was sorely frightened,
 and began aloud to shriek;
 Higher the mighty griffin
 flew, with outstretched beak;

===Chinese===
男孩惊恐万分，
放声哭喊；
威猛的狮鹫
张着长喙，越飞越高；

===Original===
 To the clouds above them floating
 he his prey was bearing.
 Sigeband, lord of Ireland,
 loudly wept, his outcries never sparing.

===Chinese===
他把猎物带向
头顶浮动的云霄。
西格班德，爱尔兰之主，
放声痛哭，哀号不绝。

===Original===
His friends and all his kinsmen
 the sorry tale soon heard;
 They, in the death of his offspring,
 his bitter sorrow shared.

===Chinese===
他的友人与全体亲族
很快听见了这伤心事；
他们在他孩子的死讯中，
与他同担悲恸。

===Original===
 Downcast were he and his lady,
 and all their loss felt nearly;
 Sorely they wept together,
 mourning the boy, now torn from them so early.

===Chinese===
他与夫人都垂首丧气，
把这丧失感同身受；
他们相偕痛哭，
哀悼那早早被夺走的孩子。

===Original===
In this their mood so gloomy,
 the happy, merry plays
 Must now be sadly ended.
 Before their frightened gaze,

===Chinese===
在他们这般阴郁的心绪里，
欢乐嬉戏的盛会
只得凄然收场。
在他们惊惶的目光前，

===Original===
 The griffin so had robbed them
 that all for home now started,
 Sober, and filled with sadness.
 They truly felt forlorn, and heavy-hearted.

===Chinese===
狮鹫这般劫夺了他们，
众人便都动身回家，
神志恹然，满怀悲伤。
他们真切地感到孤苦无依，心头沉重。

===Original===
The king was bitterly weeping,
 his breast with tears was wet;
 The high-born queen besought him
 his sorrows to forget,

===Chinese===
国王悲泣不已，
胸前泪水尽湿；
高贵的王后恳劝他
将忧伤忘却，

===Original===
 Thus wisely to him speaking:
 “Should all in death be stricken,
 There must be an end of all things;
 it is the will of God their lives hath taken.”

===Chinese===
如此明智地对他说：
「纵使万物皆殒于死亡，
万事也终须有个尽头；
取走他们性命的，是上帝的旨意。」

===Original===
Now all would hence be faring,
 but the queen to them did say:
 “I beg you, knights and warriors,
 longer with us to stay;

===Chinese===
这时众人都想启程归去，
王后却对他们说道：
「我恳求你们，骑士与武士们，
再稍作盘桓；

===Original===
 Our gifts of gold and silver,
 that here for you are ready,
 You should not think of meanly;
 our love for you is ever true and steady.”

===Chinese===
我们备下的金银赠礼，
正在此处为你们候着，
你们莫要小看了它们；
我们对你们的心意永远真挚不移。」

===Original===
The knights to her bowed lowly,
 and then began they all
 To say how they were thankful.
 The king, thereon, did call

===Chinese===
骑士们向她深深躬身，
随后众人
都称道心中的感激。
国王接着便吩咐

===Original===
 For silken stuffs, the richest,
 for all who there yet tarried;
 They had ne’er been cut nor opened;
 and from far-off lands had erst to the king been carried.

===Chinese===
取来至贵的丝绸，
分给尚未离去的众人；
那些丝绸从未经裁剪开启，
原是早先从远方之地为王运来。

===Original===
He gave them also horses,
 both palfreys and war-steeds;
 The horses out of Ireland
 were tall and of hardy breeds.

===Chinese===
他还赠与他们马匹，
既有乘用马也有战马；
爱尔兰所出的骏马
躯干高大，品种耐劳。

===Original===
 Red gold was likewise given,
 and silver without weighing;
 The king with care had bidden
 outfit good for his guests, no longer staying.

===Chinese===
红金也一并相赠，
白银不问斤两；
国王悉心吩咐，
为即别的宾客备下良好的行装。

===Original===
Soon as the queen was willing,
 each her leave now takes,
 Both lovely maids and women;
 each one herself bedecks

===Chinese===
王后一经首肯，
各人便来告辞，
美丽的少女与妇人们皆是如此；
人人盛妆打扮，

===Original===
 With gifts that made her fairer;
 all new clothes are wearing.
 The high times now are ended;
 Sigeband’s land they leave, and are homeward faring.

===Chinese===
以添姿增色的赠礼妆扮；
个个身穿新衣。
盛会欢情至此而尽；
他们辞别西格班德的国土，各自踏上归途。

## Tale II How Hagen Slew the Griffin / 第二诗章 哈根如何诛杀狮鹫

===Original===
Hagen, falling from the grasp of one of the young griffins, crawls into a cave, where he finds three young daughters of kings, who had also been carried off by the griffins. He grows up in their company. At last, one day, he wanders with them to the seashore, where he finds a ship whose master, a lord from Karadie, he persuades to rescue them from their exile.

===Chinese===
哈根（Hagen）从一只幼狮鹫（griffin）的爪中跌落，爬进一处岩窟，在那里遇见三位同样被狮鹫掳来的年少王家女儿。他在她们的陪伴中长大。终于有一天，他与她们一同游荡到海边，发现一艘船，并说服船主——一位来自卡拉迪（Karadie）的君侯——救她们脱离流放之地。

===Original===
Of how their stay was ended
 I will speak no longer here;
 Now I tell you further
 of the rushing flight in the air,

===Chinese===
他们的逗留如何收场，
我在此处不再多言；
如今我要进一步讲述
那凌空疾逝的飞翔，

===Original===
 That the child with the angry griffin
 far away was bearing.
 For this his friends and kinsmen
 long in their hearts were heavy sorrow wearing.

===Chinese===
盛怒的狮鹫驮着那孩子，
飞往遥远的他乡；
为此他的亲朋与族人
心中久久穿着沉重的悲伤。

===Original===
Because the Lord so willed it
 the child was not yet dead;
 But, none the less, he later
 a life of sadness led,

===Chinese===
只因主的旨意如此，
那孩子尚未身亡；
只是此后，
他度着凄楚愁惨的时光，

===Original===
 After the harsh old griffin
 back to his nestlings bore him.
 When on their prey they gloated,
 hard toil enough the boy had now before him.

===Chinese===
后来那暴戾的老狮鹫
把他驮回巢中幼雏身旁；
雏鸟们对着猎物垂涎，
这孩子眼前的苦役正长。

===Original===
Soon as the bird that bore him
 did on his nest alight,
 He dropped the boy he carried,
 and in his claws held tight;

===Chinese===
驮着他的大鸟刚刚
落上自家的巢窠，
便把衔来的男孩一丢，
利爪仍紧紧攥着；

===Original===
 One of the young ones caught him:
 that he did not devour him
 Thanks to God thereafter
 were given, far and wide, for the watch kept o’er him.

===Chinese===
一只幼狮鹫把他攫住：
而他未被吞入腹中，
全赖上帝随后的看顾，
感恩之声传遍西东；

===Original===
Else the birds had slain him,
 and with their claws had torn.
 Now listen all with wonder,
 and his bitter sorrow learn:

===Chinese===
否则群鸟早杀了他，
用利爪把他撕成碎片。
诸位且怀着惊奇来听，
领教他愁苦有多深：

===Original===
 Hear how the king of Ireland
 then from death was shielded;
 Him a young bird now carried,
 strongly clutched, and naught of his grip he yielded.

===Chinese===
且听爱尔兰之王
当时如何得脱死亡；
一只幼狮鹫此刻攫起他，
攥得死紧，绝不相让；

===Original===
From tree to tree in the forest
 he with the boy took flight;
 The bird a little too boldly
 trusted his strength and might.

===Chinese===
它带着这男孩
在林间逐树飞掠；
这鸟儿未免过于
自负它的膂力气魄；

===Original===
 Upon a branch he lighted,
 but now to the ground must flutter,
 For he was much too heavy;
 in the nest to have longer staid had methinks been better.

===Chinese===
它落上一根树枝，
却只得扑坠到地上，
只因它实在重过了头；
依我看，留在巢里倒更稳当。

===Original===
The child, while the bird was falling,
 broke from him away,
 And hid among the bushes,
 a little, lorn estray;

===Chinese===
孩子趁那鸟坠跌之际，
从它爪中挣脱，
藏进了灌木丛里，
一个孤苦伶仃的小小迷途者；

===Original===
 Well-nigh was he to starving,
 ’twas long since food he tasted.
 Yet on a day long after
 the hopes of women in Ireland on him rested.

===Chinese===
他几乎要活活饿死，
许久不曾沾过饮食。
然而很久以后的一天，
爱尔兰的妇人们仍把希望寄托在他身上。

===Original===
God doth many a wonder,
 truly one may say.
 By the craft of the mighty griffin,
 it came to pass one day,

===Chinese===
上帝常行奇迹，
这话千真万确；
藉着猛鸷狮鹫的爪牙，
竟有这样一天来到：

===Original===
 Three daughters fair of princes
 had been taken thither,
 And now near by were dwelling.
 No man can tell how there they lived together,

===Chinese===
三位王侯的美丽女儿
曾被掳到了那边，
如今就住在近旁。
她们如何在彼处一同过活，无人能言，

===Original===
And how, thro’ days so many,
 their lives to them were spared,
 Were it not that God in heaven
 for them in kindness cared.

===Chinese===
也没人说得清，历经
这许多日夜，她们性命如何保全——
若非天上的上帝
仁慈地把她们顾怜。

===Original===
 Hagen now no longer
 need live without a fellow;
 Those good and lovely maidens
 soon found the little waif in a rocky hollow.

===Chinese===
哈根从此
不必再孤单无伴；
那善良可爱的少女们
很快在岩窟里寻见这小小的流浪儿。

===Original===
When, crawling to his hiding,
 they the child did see,
 It might, so thought the maidens,
 a dwarfish goblin be,

===Chinese===
当她们爬近他的藏身处，
一眼看见这孩子，
少女们心中暗想：
这莫不是个矮小的妖精，

===Original===
 Or perhaps it was a water-oaf,
 from out the sea up-driven;
 But when the boy came near them,
 at once a welcome kind to him was given.

===Chinese===
又或是个水中妖魔，
被大海驱赶上岸；
待男孩走近前来，
她们当即给了他亲切的接待。

===Original===
Hagen was ware of the maidens,
 as into their cave they stole,
 While with fear and sadness
 their little hearts were full,

===Chinese===
哈根察觉了少女们，
见她们悄悄潜回岩窟，
那时恐惧与哀愁
正涨满她们幼小的心胸，

===Original===
 Before they yet had knowledge
 that they a Christian greeted.
 But the care they later showed him
 lifted the pain from many hearts o’erweighted.

===Chinese===
她们还不知道
迎来的原是基督徒一名；
但她们后来对他的照拂，
卸下了多少心头重压的苦痛。

===Original===
First spake the eldest maiden:
 “How darest thou in our cave,
 Where from the God of heaven
 we home and shelter have?

===Chinese===
年长的少女先开口：
「你怎敢闯进我们的岩窟？
我们在这儿靠着
天上上帝，才有家宅庇护。

===Original===
 Go, seek again thy playmates,
 the billowy waters under;
 Enough ourselves we sorrow,
 and on our bitter lot in sadness ponder.”

===Chinese===
去，去找你的玩伴吧，
到那波涛滚滚的水底；
我们自身的愁苦已够，
还得在悲哀里把苦命细思。」

===Original===
The high-born child then answered:
 “I pray you let me stay;
 I truly am a Christian,
 you must not say me nay.

===Chinese===
高贵的孩子回答道：
「求你们容我留下；
我实在是个基督徒，
你们千万别说回绝话。

===Original===
 One of the griffins seized me,
 and to the cave did carry;
 I cannot live all lonely,
 and here with you would I most gladly tarry.”

===Chinese===
一只狮鹫把我攫来，
掳进了这个洞天；
我无法孤身独活，
在这儿同你们相守我才情愿。」

===Original===
Then to the child so friendless
 they loving welcome gave;
 But they of his worth thereafter
 did better knowledge have.

===Chinese===
于是她们向这无依无靠的孩子
慈爱地表示欢迎；
至于他的人品才具，
她们日后才有更深的认清；

===Original===
 They now could ask him only,
 whence he had been stolen;
 But, such was then his hunger,
 in telling his tale, his heart was full and swollen.

===Chinese===
她们当时只能问他，
是被人从哪里劫来；
可他饥饿已极，
诉说身世时心头胀满辛酸。

===Original===
Then spake the little foundling:
 “Food I sorely need;
 Give to me, in kindness,
 a little drink and bread.

===Chinese===
小弃儿便说道：
「我迫切需要吃食；
请你们行个好，
给我一点水喝、一点面包。

===Original===
 ’Tis long since I have had it,
 and now three days I’m fasting,
 The while the griffin bore me,
 and full a hundred miles was hither hasting.”

===Chinese===
我有许久不曾进食，
如今已饿了整整三天，
狮鹫驮着我一路飞来，
足足飞了百里才到这边。」

===Original===
Then answered one of the maidens:
 “Our lot it so hath been,
 That we our wonted cup-bearers
 never here have seen;

===Chinese===
一位少女答道：
「我们命该如此：
惯常侍奉的司酒侍从，
在这里从未见过；

===Original===
 Neither our lordly steward,
 who should food to us be giving.”
 Still they praised God’s goodness;
 altho’ their years were few, they were wisely living.

===Chinese===
堂堂的总管也不见踪影，
本该由他供奉饮食。」
她们仍称颂上帝的恩慈；
虽然年岁尚幼，却活得明智。

===Original===
A search they soon were making
 for roots and herbage wild,
 Wherewith they hoped to strengthen
 Sigeband’s darling child.

===Chinese===
她们随即四处寻访
野生的根茎与草叶，
盼着借此滋养
西格班德（Sigeband）的娇儿气血。

===Original===
 Such food as they had lived on
 they gave to him most freely;
 To him ’twas a meal unwonted,
 but such as they long time had eaten daily.

===Chinese===
她们自己赖以活命的吃食，
毫不吝惜地分给了他；
于他这是顿稀罕的饭食，
她们却早已日复一日吃惯了它。

===Original===
Yet he needs must eat it,
 for hunger sore he hath,
 And hard it is to any
 to meet with bitter death.

===Chinese===
可他不得不下咽，
因为饥火烧灼难当；
毕竟对任何人来说，
迎向惨死都是艰难一场。

===Original===
 Thro’ all the days so dreary,
 while with the maidens dwelling,
 To them his help most willing
 he ever gave, his thankfulness thus telling.

===Chinese===
在那凄苦漫长的岁月里，
他与少女们同住一起，
总是甘心情愿地帮衬她们，
以此诉说着心中的谢意。

===Original===
They, too, had him in keeping,
 that can I say for truth;
 He there grew up in sadness,
 throughout his early youth;

===Chinese===
她们也把他照看，
这话我敢说属实；
他在那儿满怀愁苦地长大，
度过了整个少年时日；

===Original===
 Until, one day, the children,
 to make them greatly sorrow,
 Before their cavern-dwelling
 saw wonders rise, that threatened more to-morrow.

===Chinese===
直到有一天，孩子们
在岩窟居所的门前
看见奇景涌起，令他们大为哀愁，
还预示着明日更多的祸殃。

===Original===
I know not from what border,
 tossing o’er sea to land,
 Came to those shores so rocky
 a holy pilgrim band.

===Chinese===
我说不清从哪方疆土，
一路颠簸渡海而来，
一队圣洁的朝圣者
登上了那岩石嶙峋的海岸。

===Original===
 The ground-swell it was heavy,
 and rocked the bark full sorely;
 Thereat the banished maidens
 felt their care and sorrow growing hourly.

===Chinese===
海底的涌浪沉重，
把小船颠簸得厉害；
流放中的少女们眼见，
忧愁随着时辰逐刻增长。

===Original===
Soon the ship was shattered;
 not one his life could save.
 Quickly the stern old griffins
 came down beside the wave;

===Chinese===
不久船便撞得粉碎；
无一人生还逃命。
那凶悍的老狮鹫们
迅疾扑降到波涛之滨；

===Original===
 Seizing many drowned ones,
 back to their nest they hurried.
 Many a woman was mourning,
 soon as the sorry tale to her was carried.

===Chinese===
攫起许多溺亡的人，
匆匆赶回巢中。
这凄惨的消息一经传到，
多少妇人哀恸失声。

===Original===
When to the hungry nestlings
 the food they took in haste,
 Back again the griffins
 came from their offspring’s nest;

===Chinese===
待它们把食物急忙
送到嗷嗷待哺的雏鸟跟前，
狮鹫们又离开儿女的巢，
重新飞回这边；

===Original===
 From what far spot I know not,
 along the sea-paths flying.
 Their young they left on the hillside,
 with a neighbor grim, while they were hither hieing.

===Chinese===
从何等远地我不得而知，
它们沿着海路翱翔，
把雏鸟留在山坡上托给凶邻，
自己匆匆赶往这方。

===Original===
One day the goods of the sailors
 Hagen saw near the sea,
 For many had been drowned there;
 holy men were they.

===Chinese===
一天，哈根在海边
望见水手们的遗物，
因为许多人溺毙在那里；
他们都是圣洁之徒。

===Original===
 He thought, among the wreckage,
 food might still lie hidden;
 But, through fear of the wicked griffins,
 he softly crept to the shore, by hunger bidden.

===Chinese===
他想，沉船残骸之中
或许还藏着吃食；
只因惧怕那邪恶的狮鹫，
他受了饥饿的驱使，蹑足爬向岸边。

===Original===
No one could he find there,
 but a body in armor alone;
 Thereby the wild old griffin
 hard work would give him soon.

===Chinese===
他在那儿寻不到一个活人，
只见一具披甲的尸首；
那只狂野的老狮鹫
很快就要让他尝到苦头。

===Original===
 Out from his armor he shook him,
 nor did he spurn to wear it:
 He found a bow and weapons,
 by its side, on the sandy shore, lying near it.

===Chinese===
他把尸身从甲胄里抖出，
也不嫌弃，径自披挂：
又寻得一张弓和兵器，
就在沙岸上、躺近尸身旁。

===Original===
With these himself he girded,
 that simple little child;
 When in the air above him
 he heard a rushing wild.

===Chinese===
那纯朴的小小孩童
拿它们把自己束装；
忽听头顶的长空里
一阵狂野的呼啸作响。

===Original===
 He wished that he had loitered,
 the sorry little master;
 But quickly came the griffin;
 to the sheltering cavern fain would he flee the faster.

===Chinese===
这可怜的小主人
只悔方才不曾拖延；
狮鹫转眼便扑到；
他恨不得更快逃进藏身的岩窟间。

===Original===
The bird swung down in anger
 to the sandy beach and foam;
 The little playmate and fellow
 of the young it left at home,

===Chinese===
那大鸟挟着怒气
盘旋扑向沙滩与浪沫；
它先前留在家里的
幼狮鹫的小伙伴一个，

===Original===
 Would by the angry griffin
 have at once been swallowed;
 But now the bold young Hagen
 the ways of a daring foeman bravely followed.

===Chinese===
眼看要被狂怒的狮鹫
当场一口吞掉；
但年少胆壮的哈根
勇敢地施出了剽悍敌手的狠招。

===Original===
He with strength but youthful
 the tightened string drew out,
 And arrows swift and many
 from the well-bent bow he shot.

===Chinese===
他以少年的膂力
将绷紧的弓弦拉满，
从那张良弓上
射出许多支疾飞的箭。

===Original===
 Alas! he did not hit him;
 what hope of his ill-luck turning?
 Then he of the sword bethought him;
 he heard the maids bewailing him and mourning.

===Chinese===
哎呀！他没能射中；
厄运可有转圜之望？
这时他想起了剑；
听得少女们正为他哀哭悲伤。

===Original===
Though his years were not yet many,
 he still was brave enough;
 A wing from the angry griffin
 he struck at the shoulder off,

===Chinese===
他虽年岁尚幼，
胆气却足够刚强；
挥剑把那狂怒的狮鹫
自肩头斩落一只翅膀，

===Original===
 And in the leg he smote him
 a heavier blow and stronger;
 So that his wounded body
 the bird away from the spot could drag no longer.

===Chinese===
又朝它的腿上，
给了更沉重的一击；
那鸟负伤的身躯
再也拖不动自己远离此地。

===Original===
The boy was now the winner;
 one of his foes lay dead;
 But quickly came another,
 who sorrow for him made.

===Chinese===
男孩这一场得胜；
一个对头已然毙命；
可另一只旋即扑来，
又给他添了愁惊。

===Original===
 All at last were slaughtered;
 nor old nor young were living;
 God in heaven helped him;
 but truly against such strength ’twere hopeless striving.

===Chinese===
到末了全数被诛戮，
老的少的无一幸存；
天上的上帝佑助了他；
说实话，对抗这般蛮力本似无望之争。

===Original===
When he that feat of wonder
 had done, with heart so brave,
 He called the friendly maidens
 from out their rocky cave.

===Chinese===
当他以豪勇之心
完成了这桩奇迹，
便把友善的少女们
从岩窟中唤出。

===Original===
 He said: “Let air and sunshine
 your sorry hearts be filling;
 Since now the God in heaven
 to grant to us some bliss at last is willing.”

===Chinese===
他说道：「让空气与阳光
灌满你们愁苦的心怀；
因为天上的上帝
终于肯赐我们几分欢快。」

===Original===
His call they kindly welcomed,
 and many times, forsooth,
 The boy by the lovely maidens
 was kissed upon the mouth.

===Chinese===
她们亲切地应承他的呼唤，
而且，说实话，不止一回，
那可爱的少女们
把这男孩亲吻在唇上。

===Original===
 Their keeper now lay lifeless;
 and none there was to hinder
 Their roaming o’er the hillsides,
 and, far or near, at their goodwill to wander.

===Chinese===
看守她们的恶物如今僵卧；
再没有人来阻拦
她们遍游山坡，
或远或近，随心流连。

===Original===
By help of the boy, from sorrow
 they now were wholly free;
 The little childish wanderer,
 so skilled with the bow was he,

===Chinese===
多亏这男孩，她们
如今全然脱却了忧伤；
这小小年纪的浪游儿
一张弓使得多娴熟在行，

===Original===
 That birds his well-shot arrows
 could never shun by flying.
 He shot them now for pastime;
 but to get them soon for food must he be trying.

===Chinese===
飞鸟再快，也躲不过
他射出的一支支好箭。
此时射鸟不过消遣；
不久他就得为口粮连番猎狩。

===Original===
He in heart was daring,
 he was mild, but also brave;
 Hey! from the wild beasts learning,
 what nimble leaps he gave!

===Chinese===
他天性勇猛，
温良而又英豪；
嘿！从野兽学得本领，
瞧他蹦跳得何等轻巧！

===Original===
 As doth the strong young panther,
 over the rocks he scrambled;
 Himself was his only teacher,
 and, far away from kin, alone he rambled.

===Chinese===
好比壮健的幼豹，
他在岩间攀腾；
自己就是唯一的师尊，
远离亲族，独自驰骋。

===Original===
While on the shore, by the waters,
 his time he often spent,
 He saw, among the sea-waves,
 live fishes, as he went;

===Chinese===
他常在水滨海岸
消磨他的时光，
行走间只见海浪里
活鱼游动成行；

===Original===
 To catch them it were easy,
 but yet he did not get them,
 For with fire his kitchen smoked not.
 Daily his sorrow grew that he could not eat them.

===Chinese===
捉它们本属容易，
他却始终没有去捉，
因为他的厨房无火起烟。
不能吃上它们，他的愁烦逐日增多。

===Original===
Oft from his rocky shelter
 to the forest he would roam;
 Many wild beasts saw he,
 strong and grim in their home.

===Chinese===
他常从岩窟栖身之处
信步走进林莽；
见过多少野兽，
在巢穴里强横而狰狞的模样。

===Original===
 One there was among them
 greedy to devour him;
 But with his sword he slew him,
 and let him quickly feel the hate he bore him.

===Chinese===
其中有一头
馋涎欲滴要把他吞吃；
他挥剑将它斩杀，
让它立时尝到他恨意的滋味。

===Original===
Unto a wild chameleon
 this dreadful thing was like;
 Its skin the boy drew from it,
 (for that was he not too weak;)

===Chinese===
这骇人的东西
活像一头野生的变色龙；
男孩把它的皮剥下
（这力气他倒不缺用；）

===Original===
 Now for its blood he thirsted,
 and, when of this he had taken,
 He felt great strength come o’er him;
 and many thoughts began in him to waken.

===Chinese===
他随即渴求它的血，
待到喝下之后，
只觉神力涌遍周身；
种种念头也在心中苏醒不休。

===Original===
Then with the skin of the monster
 he wrapped himself around;
 When soon to him it happened
 hard by a lion he found.

===Chinese===
于是他拿这怪兽的皮
把周身紧紧裹住；
不多时他竟撞见
一头狮子近在咫尺。

===Original===
 To shun him it were hopeless,
 for he quickly rushed upon him;
 But the boy was yet unwounded;
 his foe from the daring child warm welcome won him.

===Chinese===
躲避已全无指望，
它迅猛地扑上前来；
男孩却毫发未损；
那仇敌从这大胆的孩子领受了热辣的接待。

===Original===
When he the lion had smitten
 to death, with many blows,
 He to the cave would take it,
 as homeward thence he goes.

===Chinese===
待他连连挥击、
把狮子打死之后，
便把它扛回岩窟，
一路踏上归途。

===Original===
 At all times had the maidens
 been by his care upholden,
 But now this food unwonted
 did raise their waning strength, and their hearts embolden.

===Chinese===
少女们向来
靠他照料扶持；
这顿罕见的美食
又使她们衰减的气力重振、胆气复滋。

===Original===
Of fire they yet knew nothing,
 but wood they need not seek;
 From out a stone he quickly
 many sparks did strike.

===Chinese===
他们尚且不识火，
柴薪也无须寻觅；
他很快从一块石头上
敲出纷纷的火星。

===Original===
 The food they long had wanted
 he soon was on them bestowing,
 And, since there was none to do it,
 themselves the flesh must cook on the coals now glowing.

===Chinese===
他们久盼的吃食，
他很快便向他们分赠；
只是无人代为操持，
他们只得自己在红炭上把肉烤成。

===Original===
When they of food had eaten,
 at once they grew more strong;
 Their boldness, too, grew greater,
 (to God their thanks belong.)

===Chinese===
用过这顿饭食，
他们登时强健起来；
胆气也愈发壮盛
（感谢当归于上帝。）

===Original===
 And now their bodies also
 as healthy were, and comely,
 As if they still were living,
 each in her fatherland, on fare more homely.

===Chinese===
如今他们的身体
又这般康健姣好，
仿佛仍各自生活在父邦，
吃着家常的粗淡饭食。

===Original===
The wild young Hagen also
 the strength of twelve did own;
 And for this, thro’ all his lifetime,
 praise by him was won.

===Chinese===
野性的少年哈根
更怀有十二人的膂力；
为此他终其一生，
都赢得了世人的称誉。

===Original===
 But both to him and the maidens
 ’twas pain and sorrow only,
 To think that they forever
 must pass their lives in a waste so sad and lonely.

===Chinese===
只是对他和少女们来说，
唯有愁苦与悲伤，
一想到此生永远
要在凄凉孤寂的荒野中消磨时光。

===Original===
They begged of him to lead them
 down to the watery flood.
 Shame they felt in going,
 for the clothes were none too good

===Chinese===
她们恳求他把她们
带下山去，到那大水之滨；
临行又自觉羞惭，
只因她们身上穿的衣裙

===Original===
 The maidens now were wearing;
 they themselves had sewed them,
 Ere yet the youthful Hagen
 them in their banishment found, and his kindness showed them.

===Chinese===
实在算不得体面；
那是她们亲手缝就，
远在年轻的哈根
在流放中寻见她们、施以恩惠之前。

===Original===
For days full four and twenty
 they fared thro’ the piny wood;
 At last, on a morning early,
 down they came to the flood,

===Chinese===
整整二十四天，
他们赶路穿过松林；
终于在一个清晨，
下到了那片大水之滨，

===Original===
 And saw a laden galley,
 that came from Garadé.
 Then did the lonely maidens
 sorrow and pain at the sailors’ plight betray.

===Chinese===
望见一艘满载的帆船，
自加拉迪（Garadé）驶来。
孤独的少女们见了，
不禁为水手们的险境流露忧戚。

===Original===
Hagen shouted loudly;
 he was hindered none the more,
 Altho’ the winds were boisterous,
 and wild the waves did roar.

===Chinese===
哈根高声呼喊；
他并未因此受半分阻拦，
尽管狂风呼啸，
巨浪滔天。

===Original===
 Now the ship was groaning;
 and the sailors, landward steering,
 Felt dread of water-nixes,
 on seeing the maids, as they the shore were nearing.

===Chinese===
这时船身呻吟；
水手们朝陆地掌舵而行，
望见少女们时心怀水妖之惧，
一面渐渐驶近岸边。

===Original===
The ship it had a master,
 a lord from out Salmé;
 Hagen, as well as his kindred,
 had he known on a former day.

===Chinese===
这船有位船主，
是萨尔梅（Salmé）来的一位君侯；
哈根连同他的亲族，
他从前本是旧识。

===Original===
 They before were neighbors,
 but Ireland’s child, here roaming,
 The youthful son of Sigeband,
 was to the pilgrims unknown, who now were coming.

===Chinese===
他们从前互为近邻；
但爱尔兰之子、
西格班德年少的儿郎，
对这些新来的朝圣者却是面生。

===Original===
The earl forbade his steersman
 nearer to sail to the shore;
 But now the childish outcast
 but begged of them the more,

===Chinese===
伯爵喝止他的舵手，
不许再向岸边驶近；
可这年幼的流落儿
反而愈发苦苦求恳，

===Original===
 For love of God, to take them
 away from that shore forsaken.
 The sailors felt emboldened,
 when by the boy the name of Christ was taken.

===Chinese===
求他们看在上帝的分上，
带他们离开那被弃的海岸。
听得男孩口中念出基督之名，
水手们顿觉胆壮心安。

===Original===
The earl, with eleven others,
 into a boat now sprung;
 Ere he the truth was learning,
 the time to him seemed long.

===Chinese===
伯爵带着十一名随从，
纵身跃上小艇；
在查明真相之前，
他只觉时光漫长难等。

===Original===
 Whether the maidens as goblins
 or mermaids must be treated
 He knew not; such beings never,
 in all his life before, his eyes had greeted.

===Chinese===
这些少女该当作妖精
还是美人鱼看待，
他委实不知；这般生灵，
他生平从未亲眼见过。

===Original===
He first began to ask them,
 before he reached the strand:
 “Boy, have you been baptized?
 What do you in this land?”

===Chinese===
他还未登上沙滩，
便先开口动问：
「孩子，你可受过洗礼？
你在这片土地上做什么？」

===Original===
 Dight with fresh green mosses
 he saw those lovely daughters,
 Who earnestly begged the sailors
 that they would deign to take them o’er the waters.

===Chinese===
他望见那些可爱的少女，
身披鲜绿的新苔，
正恳切地哀求水手们，
俯允载她们渡过这片大海。

## Tale III How Hagen Sailed to His Home / 第三诗章 哈根扬帆归故里

===Original===
The master of the ship inquires of Hagen and the maidens concerning their country, and learns that Hagen is the son of an old enemy. He threatens to keep him as a hostage, but Hagen seizes thirty of the sailors, and flings them into the sea, and compels the others to steer towards his home. There, he sends messengers to announce his arrival to his father and mother, who come down to the shore to meet him.

===Chinese===
船主向哈根（Hagen）与众少女询问她们的故国，从而得知哈根乃旧日仇敌之子。他威胁要将哈根扣为人质；哈根却一把揪住三十名水手，把他们掷入海中，迫使其余众人把船驶向他的家乡。抵达之后，他派使者向父母通报自己归来的消息，二老于是亲赴岸边相迎。

===Original===
Ere they went on shipboard, the pilgrims them besought
Kindly to take the clothing they with them had brought.
However shy were the maidens, to wear them they were ready;
They donned the clothes with blushes, and now their sorrow had an ending speedy.

===Chinese===
登船之前，朝圣的旅人们好意相请，
求她们收下自己随身带来的衣裳；
少女们虽然羞怯，却也愿意穿戴，
她们红晕满颊地换上新衣，忧愁迅即有了收场。

===Original===
Soon as the lovely maidens embarked upon the wave,
They heartily were greeted by knights both good and brave,
Who to the high-born daughters welcome to give were heedful;
Though they at first mistook them, and thought them wicked elves, or mermaids dreadful.

===Chinese===
可爱的少女们刚一登上海浪中的船，
便受到良善勇毅的骑士们热诚的欢迎；
骑士们一心要礼遇这些出身高贵的闺秀，
虽然起初他们错把她们当作邪恶的精灵，或是骇人的美人鱼。

===Original===
That night the maidens rested with friends upon the sea;
So wondrous was their dwelling, from fear they were not free:
Wiser it were in the children to think this home a blessing.
Soon as the earl had bidden, their food upon the maids they all were pressing.

===Chinese===
当夜，少女们在海上与友伴同眠；
这居处如此奇异，恐惧未从心头驱散：
这些孩子若能把此处当作福地，方为明智。
伯爵一声吩咐，众人便争相向少女们殷勤劝餐。

===Original===
After they had eaten, and while with them he sat,
The lord of the land of Garadie the maidens did entreat
To say by whom such fair ones were brought unto that shore.
The children, at his asking, only felt their sorrow grow the more.

===Chinese===
用饭既毕，他还坐在她们近旁，
加拉迪（Garadie）之主便恳请少女们相告，
是何人把这般佳人送到这片海岸。
孩子们经他一问，只觉愁绪愈发滋长。

===Original===
First answered him the eldest of those who with him sat:
“I come from a far-off kingdom, (my lord, now hear my fate;)
I was born in the land of India, a land wherein my father
Was king while he was living, but I, alas! the crown must leave to another.”

===Chinese===
座中年纪最长的少女先答他问：
「我来自一个遥远的国度（吾主，且听我的命运；）
我生在印度之地，在那片国土上，我的父亲
在世时曾为君王；而我，唉！王冠只得让与他人。」

===Original===
Then spake the maid next younger: “I too have come from far;
Erewhile a strong old griffin did me from Portugal bear.
A king in the land was my father; none than he was prouder,
Nor for a mighty ruler, far or near, were ever praises louder.”

===Chinese===
接着次幼的少女开言：「我也来自远方；
昔日一只强壮的老狮鹫将我从葡萄牙攫走。
我父曾是那片土地上的君王；无人比他更傲岸，
无论远近的强大君王，得到的赞誉都不及他响亮。」

===Original===
Then the youngest maiden, who by the earl sat near,
To him spoke low and modestly, and said: “I pray you hear;
From Iserland I was carried, my father there held power;
But from those who hoped to rear me, alas! afar was I borne in an evil hour.”

===Chinese===
最年幼的少女坐在伯爵身旁，
低声而谦恭地对他言道：「请您听我讲：
我被人从伊瑟兰（Iserland）掳走，我父在那里执掌权柄；
可是，唉！凶险的时辰里，我被带离想养育我的人，流落远方。」

===Original===
The high-born knight then answered: “By God ’tis ordered well,
Since you among your kinsfolk not long were left to dwell;
Now, at last, by his kindness you are freed from dangers,
For I within these borders have found you living here, such lovely strangers.”

===Chinese===
高贵的骑士答道：「这是上帝妥善的安排，
你们在亲族之中未得久住；
如今终于蒙他的仁慈，你们脱离了种种凶险，
因为我在这些疆界之内遇见了活着的你们——如此可爱的异乡佳丽。」

===Original===
However much he asked them, they yet to tell were loath,
How unto them it happened grim death had spared them both,
When erewhiles the griffin unto his nest had brought them.
Many had been their sorrows; no more to speak of these the maids bethought them.

===Chinese===
任凭他怎样追问，她们总不愿讲述，
那狰狞的死神如何偏偏放过了她们俩，
当年狮鹫又如何把她们掳回巢中。
她们苦难重重；少女们决意不再提起这些伤痛。

===Original===
Then said the worthy leader, turning to the youth;
“My dearest friend and fellow, now let me hear the truth;
Since unto me these maidens their sorry tale have given,
From you would I hear gladly, and learn the land and kin whence you were riven.”

===Chinese===
尊贵的首领于是转向那少年，说道：
「我最亲爱的朋友和伙伴，请让我听闻实情；
这些少女已向我讲了自己悲惨的遭遇，
我也乐意听你道来：你从哪片国土、哪门亲族中被生生撕离。」

===Original===
To him wild Hagen answered: “That will I tell to you;
One of those dreadful griffins bore me hither too.
Sigeband was my father; in Ireland once was I living;
But long with these lovely maidens I since have dwelt, with many sorrows striving.”

===Chinese===
狂野的哈根答道：「这我愿说与你听；
也是那些可怖狮鹫中的一只，把我掳到了此地。
西格班德（Sigeband）是我的父亲；我从前住在爱尔兰；
但此后许久，我与这些可爱的少女同住，与重重忧患抗争。」

===Original===
Then they all besought him to say how it befell
That, living with the griffins he had come off so well.
To them young Hagen answered: “To God it all was owing;
But now I have cooled my anger; no more for them my heart with hate is glowing.”

===Chinese===
众人于是一齐恳求他讲个分明，
与狮鹫同处，他怎能保全自身。
年轻的哈根回答他们：「这一切全赖上天；
如今我的怒气已消，心中不再为它们燃着仇恨。」

===Original===
Then spake the lord of Garadie: “I fain would learn from you
How you were freed from danger?” He said: “I quickly slew
Both the old and the young ones; not one of those is living
By whom my life was threatened, and who to me such fear were daily giving.”

===Chinese===
加拉迪之主又开口：「我倒想听你说说，
你是怎样脱离险境？」他说：「我手起刀落，
把老的小的一并诛戮；那些家伙无一幸存——
它们威胁我的性命，日日令我惊惶不定。」

===Original===
Then said all the sailors: “Your strength indeed was great;
For every man and woman to praise you it were meet.
A thousand of us truly ’gainst them in, vain had striven,
Nor ever could have slain them; truly to you have blessings great been given.”

===Chinese===
水手们于是齐声道：「你的力量实在超群；
无论男子还是妇人，人人都该将你称颂。
说真的，我们一千人与它们相拼也是枉然，
永远休想杀死它们；上天确实赐了你巨大的鸿恩。」

===Original===
The earl and all his followers were of the boy afraid;
His strength was past all measure, and sorrow for them made.
They would by craft his weapons have taken from him gladly,
But these he sternly guarded, and soon, thro’ him, it ended for them sadly.

===Chinese===
伯爵和他的全体随从都畏惧这少年；
他的膂力超乎一切衡量，令他们忧惧交并。
他们本想用诡计快快夺下他的兵器，
他却厉加守卫；很快，经他之手，事情对他们落得悲惨收场。

===Original===
Then spake the earl yet further: “It now has happened well,
After our toilsome wanderings, and all that us befell.
But since you are a kinsman of my foeman, Sigeband,
And here have come from Ireland, I as a hostage hold you in my hand.

===Chinese===
伯爵又继续说道：「如今事态正巧，
历尽劳苦的漂泊，又遭了这许多变故。
但你既是我仇人西格班德的亲族，
又从爱尔兰来到这里，我就要把你当人质握在手中。

===Original===
“You come to me most fitly, as you shall know ere long,
For many of your kindred have done to me great wrong.
In Garadie’s fair kingdom, which lies too near their border,
In a heavy fight, my warriors were seized upon and murdered by their shameful order.”

===Chinese===
你来得正是再合适不过，不久你自会知晓，
因为你的许多亲族曾对我作下大恶。
在加拉迪美丽的王国——它离他们的边境实在太近——
一场恶战中，我的武士们遭他们无耻下令，被擒住杀害。」

===Original===
Then answered him young Hagen: “Of all the wrongs they did
I am wholly guiltless; if me to them you lead
I their hearts will soften, and so will the strife be ended.
Let hope to me be granted that I on my kinsmen’s shore may soon be landed.”

===Chinese===
年轻的哈根回答：「他们所行的种种不义，
我全然无辜；你若肯把我带到他们那里，
我自会软化他们的心，争斗也就能止息。
请容我怀着一个希望：让我早日登上亲族的海岸之地。」

===Original===
Then said the earl to Hagen: “For a pledge must you abide,
And I shall keep these maidens to live at court by my side;
They will swell my greatness, and I shall be their owner.”
Then thought the youthful Hagen, such words to be to him a wrong and a dishonor.

===Chinese===
伯爵对哈根说：「你必须留下充当抵押，
这些少女我也要留在身边，在宫中生活；
她们会增添我的威势，我将成为她们的主人。」
年轻的哈根闻言心想：这于他是欺辱与羞辱。

===Original===
He quickly said in anger: “No bondsman will I be;
That may no man ask for, who would unscathed go free.
And now, my worthy sailors, you needs to my land must bear me;
I will reward you gladly, and to give you clothes and gold will never spare me.

===Chinese===
他疾言怒道：「我绝不做人的奴仆；
谁若想安然无恙地走脱，便不该提这要求。
好了，我可敬的水手们，你们务必把我送往我的国土；
我会欣然酬谢，给你们衣物黄金，绝不吝惜分毫。

===Original===
“The earl has thought my maidens his own shall ever be;
But they shall yet be happy, and shall of him be free.
Whoe’er is blest with wisdom, let him my bidding follow;
Look to your sails, and turn them, and guide the ship to Ireland, o’er the billow.”

===Chinese===
伯爵满心以为，这些少女将永远归他所有；
但她们终会得到幸福，终将摆脱他的羁束。
凡蒙受智慧眷顾的人，请听从我的吩咐：
照看你们的船帆，转舵换向，驾船破浪，直向爱尔兰国土。」

===Original===
The men, as the earl had bidden, to seize the boy now dared,
But boldly did he meet them, and for their lives they feared.
He by the hair caught thirty, and into the water flung them;
Soon the strength of his body was known to all, and dreaded much among them.

===Chinese===
水手们奉伯爵之命，斗胆上前捉拿少年，
他却昂然迎击，他们个个自惧性命难保。
他揪住三十人的头发，把他们抛进了海水；
顷刻间他神力之名传遍众人，人人胆战心摇。

===Original===
Had not the kindly maidens sought to end the fight,
Soon the earl of Garadie he would have killed outright.
’Gainst neither low nor mighty did his anger falter;
These warriors and sailors now to Ireland’s shores their way must alter.

===Chinese===
若非善良的少女们竭力劝止这场争斗，
加拉迪伯爵早被他当场结果性命。
无论对方卑微还是显赫，他的怒气都不曾动摇；
这些武士和水手如今只得改换航路，驶向爱尔兰的海滨。

===Original===
They began at once to hasten, lest he their lives might take;
For now the wrath of Hagen made them with fear to quake.
For seventeen days the sailors from toiling never rested,
And sorely were they frightened; whene’er he seemed unkind they ills forecasted.

===Chinese===
他们随即兼程疾驶，唯恐他取了他们性命；
因为哈根的雷霆之怒，吓得他们浑身战栗。
整整十七天，水手们操劳不曾有一刻歇息，
他们惊恐万状；只要他稍显不悦，他们便预感大祸将至。

===Original===
When he now drew nearer unto his father’s shore,
He saw the roomy castles he well had known before;
Soon a lofty palace he spied at the edge of the river;
Three hundred towers fully he there beheld, as strong and good as ever.

===Chinese===
此刻他渐渐驶近父亲的国土，
望见了那些他素来熟悉的宽敞城堡；
随即他又望见河边一座巍峨的宫殿，
足足三百座塔楼尽收眼底，一如既往坚固完好。

===Original===
In it dwelt King Sigeband, with his proud and queenly wife.
Again each pilgrim sailor thought to lose his life;
For should the lord of Ireland aught of them be learning,
They feared that he would slay them; but Hagen stood between, his anger turning.

===Chinese===
宫中住着西格班德国王，和他高贵庄严的王后。
朝圣的水手们又一个个自料性命难保；
因为爱尔兰之主若对他们略有风闻，
他们害怕会遭他诛戮；幸有哈根居间，把那怒火扭转消融。

===Original===
Then spake unto the pilgrims that brave and warlike man:
“Your peace will I make gladly, altho’ I do not reign;
I hold no sway in the kingdom, but thither will I be sending,
And ’twixt yourself and my father of the hatred old I soon will make an ending.

===Chinese===
那位英武刚猛的人对朝圣者们说道：
「我乐意促成你们的平安，虽然我并未临朝；
我在王国中并无权柄，但我会向那里遣人传信，
在你与我父亲之间，那份旧日仇恨，我很快便替你们了清。

===Original===
“Would any now be doing what wealth to him will bring,
Let him my errand carry. Whoever to the king
Shall say what I shall bid him, gold will I give him truly;
And also, very gladly, my father and my mother will reward him duly.”

===Chinese===
谁若此刻愿做一桩能带来财富的事情，
就请替我跑这趟差。谁肯对国王
转达我嘱咐的话，我必真心以黄金相赠；
而且，我父亲和母亲也会非常乐意，给他应得的酬劳。」

===Original===
Twelve of the stranded pilgrims he bade to ride away:
“Now ask of the king, my father,” thus the youth did say,
“Whether to see young Hagen, his son, he still is yearning﻿—
Him who erst by the griffin was stolen far away, heart-sorrow learning.

===Chinese===
他吩咐落难的朝圣者中十二人策马先行：
「去问问国王，我的父亲，」少年这样说道，
「他是否还在渴念见到年轻的哈根——他的儿子，
那昔日被狮鹫远远掳走、尝尽心酸悲苦的孩子。

===Original===
“I know that what you tell him the king will not believe;
Then ask you of my mother if she her faith will give,
And if for her child to own me she will at last be willing,
If I upon my bosom will show a golden cross, the proof fulfilling.”

===Chinese===
我知道，你们告诉他的话，国王必不肯信；
那就去问我的母亲，问她可愿立下信约，
问她最终可愿意认我做她的孩子，
只要我当胸亮出那枚金十字，凭证便能圆满应验。」

===Original===
When those he sent had ridden farther into the land,
They found, in the palace seated, Queen U-te and Sigeband.
Then knew the king that the riders from Garadie came thither,
And that they to him were foemen; at this both he and his men were wroth together.

===Chinese===
他差去的使者们策马深入国土，
在宫中见到了端坐的乌特（U-te）王后与西格班德。
国王随即认出，来骑是加拉迪人马，
且与他为敌；为此他与臣下一齐震怒难当。

===Original===
He asked of them how dared they to come within his land?
Then one among them answered: “We are sent here at the hand
Of your son, the youthful Hagen. If any fain would meet him,
He now is here, so near you, that you ere many hours, in truth, may greet him.”

===Chinese===
他问他们：怎敢擅入他的疆土？
其中一人答道：「我们是奉您儿子、
年轻的哈根之命而来。谁若渴望与他相见，
他此刻就在近处；实情如此，不出几个时辰，您便能与他见面。」

===Original===
Then spake the kingly Sigeband: “To cheat there is no need;
The loss of my dear little one, who hath so long been dead,
Still my heart’s deep sorrow doth too oft awaken.”
“Ask, then, the queen, your lady, if for a falsehood should our word be taken?

===Chinese===
王者气度的西格班德说道：「行骗大可不必；
我那亲爱的小宝贝，死去已这么长久，
他的夭折至今仍时时搅动我心底的哀恸。」
「那么请问您的夫人王后：难道我们的话该被当作谎骗？

===Original===
“The little boy so often in her fond care has been,
She knows if on his bosom a golden cross was seen.
And if upon this wanderer be found the selfsame token,
You as your child can own him; you then will grant that truth by us is spoken.”

===Chinese===
「那孩子从前常在王后的慈爱看护之下，
她自然知道他胸前可曾见过一枚金十字。
倘若这位流浪者身上寻得同样的信物，
您便可以认他为您自己的孩子；也当承认我们句句属实。」

===Original===
Then to the Lady U-te the tale was quickly told;
Glad was she of the tidings, yet mourned she as of old.
She said: “Now let us hasten, that the truth no more be hidden.”
Her lord then bade to saddle; and steeds for himself and his bravest knights were bidden.

===Chinese===
这番话很快便传给了乌特夫人；
她闻讯又喜，却仍像往常一样悲戚。
她说：「我们快些动身，莫让真相再被隐匿。」
她的夫君随即吩咐备鞍，为他自己和最勇猛的骑士们备下坐骑。

===Original===
Straightway one of the pilgrims to the fair Queen U-te said:
“I will tell you, if you listen, what now to do you need.
You first must carry clothing for each young lovely maiden
Whose coming does you honor; as followers of your son were they hither bidden.”

===Chinese===
随即一名朝圣者对美丽的乌特王后说道：
「您若肯听，我便告诉您眼下该做什么。
您须先为每一位年轻可爱的少女备好衣装，
她们的到来于您是体面；她们是作为您儿子的随行被请到这地方。」

===Original===
Soon brought they richest clothing, and tiring-women, too;
The queen was also followed by men both brave and true.
They found the youthful Hagen, who on the shore was standing;
And many men from Garadie, who with the wandering boy on the beach were landing.

===Chinese===
很快，人们送来了华贵的衣裳，还有侍奉梳妆的妇人；
跟随王后来的，也尽是忠勇可靠之士。
他们找到了年轻的哈根，他正伫立在岸上；
还有加拉迪的许多随员，正伴着这位流浪归来的少年，在海滩登临。

## Tale IV How Hagen Was Met by His Father and Mother / 第四诗章 哈根如何与父母相会

===Original===
Hagen reconciles his father to the men who had brought him home, and receives from his father the government of the kingdom. He marries one of the three maidens, Hilda, from India. She gives birth to a daughter, likewise named Hilda. When she is grown up, her many suitors are rejected by her father.

===Chinese===
哈根（Hagen）使父亲与送他归国的人们尽释前嫌，并从父亲手中领受王国的治权。他娶了三位少女中的一位——来自印度的希尔达（Hilda）。她生下一个女儿，同样取名希尔达。女儿长大成人后，众多求婚者皆被她父亲回绝。

===Original===
Soon both men and women
 riding there were seen;
 Then the brave young Hagen
 went forth to meet his kin.

===Chinese===
只见男男女女
纷纷骑马出现在那里；
于是勇敢的少年哈根
迎上前去会见亲人。

===Original===
 Who ’twas that came to greet him
 he now to know was seeking;
 The throng grew ever thicker
 of friends who came in haste, their kindness speaking.

===Chinese===
来迎接他的究竟是谁，
他此刻正想探问明白；
匆匆赶来的朋友越聚越众，
满怀善意，络绎不绝。

===Original===
The king a friendly welcome
 into his land soon gave;
 He said: “To send men hither
 did you the boldness have,

===Chinese===
国王随即在他的国土上
向他们致以友善的欢迎；
他说：「你们竟有这样的胆量，
遣人来到此地，

===Original===
 To say that our queen beloved
 is in truth your mother?
 If the words are true you’ve spoken,
 so glad as now I am there’s not another.”

===Chinese===
说我们深爱的王后
当真是你们的母亲？
倘若你们所言属实，
再没有第二人像我此刻这般欢欣。」

===Original===
His queen, the lovely U-te,
 with lofty breeding, said:
 “Bid that for these new-comers
 lodgings now be made;

===Chinese===
他的王后，美丽的乌特（U-te），
气度雍容，开口说道：
「吩咐下去，为这些新来的客人
即刻备好住处；

===Original===
 I shall know right easily
 if he for the crown is fitted.”
 She found, in truth, the token,
 and, full of bliss, her youthful son she greeted.

===Chinese===
我立刻便能认出，
他是否当得起王冠。」
她果然认出了凭证，
欣喜万分地迎向她年轻的儿子。

===Original===
With eyes all wet with weeping,
 she kissed him on the mouth:
 “Though I before was ailing,
 I now am well in sooth.

===Chinese===
她噙着满眶的泪水，
亲吻他的嘴唇：
「虽然我先前抱恙在身，
如今确实已然痊愈。

===Original===
 Welcome be thou, my Hagen,
 my only child, loved dearly!
 All in the land of Sigeband
 right glad shall be for him they lost so early.”

===Chinese===
欢迎你，我的哈根，
我唯一的孩子、我的至爱！
西格班德（Sigeband）全境之人，
都将为这早早失散的孩子重展欢颜。」

===Original===
The king to the youth came nearer;
 his happiness was great.
 For the hearty love he bore him
 his manly cheek was wet;

===Chinese===
国王向少年走近；
他的幸福何其深浓。
出于对儿子由衷的爱，
他刚毅的面颊被泪水打湿；

===Original===
 With tears that hot had risen
 his eyes were overflowing.
 For the child, from him erst stolen,
 rightly the father felt the love he was showing.

===Chinese===
滚烫的热泪涌起，
从他眼中满溢而出。
对这当年被劫走的孩子，
父亲此刻流露的正是天性中的深爱。

===Original===
The queenly U-te welcomed
 the homeless maids that day;
 Many clothes she gave them,
 both bright in hue and gray,

===Chinese===
尊贵的王后乌特当日
接待了这些无家可归的少女；
她赠给她们许多衣裳，
既有鲜彩也有素灰，

===Original===
 Of silk, with downy linings,
 that much the maidens wanted.
 Their sorrows now were lightened
 by all the gifts the wife of Sigeband granted.

===Chinese===
皆是丝绸所制、衬着绒里，
正是少女们深所需索。
她们的愁苦就此稍得宽解，
全凭西格班德之妻所赐的种种礼物。

===Original===
Well they clothed the maidens,
 as their loveliness became;
 This they long had needed,
 and oft had blushed with shame;

===Chinese===
人们把少女们妆扮起来，
正配她们的美貌；
这是她们长久以来所缺，
还常为此羞红了脸；

===Original===
 But, decked with gaudy trimmings,
 now they came less shyly.
 The king and all his followers
 soon to the maidens gave a welcome freely.

===Chinese===
但如今缀上鲜丽的饰带，
她们露面时便不再那么羞怯。
国王与全体随从
旋即向少女们致以由衷的欢迎。

===Original===
Hagen asked that friendship
 to the men of Garadie
 Should by the king and his lieges
 be granted speedily;

===Chinese===
哈根恳请，
愿国王与封臣们
速速与加拉迪（Garadie）的人们
修好结谊；

===Original===
 Beseeching his forgiveness
 for all their foul misdoing.
 Soon, at the wish of Hagen,
 kindness to the pilgrims he was showing.

===Chinese===
并恳求他宽宥
他们全部的丑恶行径。
不久，依了哈根的心愿，
国王便向这些朝圣的旅人显示仁厚。

===Original===
When the king had kissed them
 and soothed his angry mood,
 He to the shipwrecked pilgrims
 made their losses good.

===Chinese===
待国王亲吻了他们、
平息了胸中的怒气，
他便补偿了这些遭海难的
朝圣的旅人所受的损失。

===Original===
 To them it was a blessing,
 and praise to Hagen carried;
 The lands of the men of Ireland
 never since have they as foemen harried.

===Chinese===
这对他们是一桩福泽，
他们把赞颂归于哈根；
爱尔兰人的国土，
从此再未遭他们以敌寇之姿侵掠。

===Original===
Then their food and clothing
 the guests took out, for use,
 And up on the sands they bore them,
 trusting in Hagen’s truce;

===Chinese===
随后客人们取出
食物与衣装，以备日用，
搬到沙滩之上，
信赖着哈根许下的休约；

===Original===
 To take their rest for a fortnight
 them did he embolden.
 The band of haughty pilgrims
 to give to him their thanks were now beholden.

===Chinese===
他鼓励他们安歇
整整十四天。
这一队朝圣的旅人虽则高傲，
从此也欠下了对他的一腔谢忱。

===Original===
Then, in the midst of uproar,
 they rode away from the shore;
 Up the castle of Ballian
 came also many more,

===Chinese===
随后，在一片喧腾之中，
他们骑马离开了海岸；
向巴利安（Ballian）城堡
又涌来了许许多多的人，

===Original===
 Led by a tale of wonder
 that the son of the king was living﻿—
 Of their king so rich and mighty;
 a thing so strange was to many past believing.

===Chinese===
他们是被一桩奇闻引来的，
说国王的儿子还在人间——
说的是他们那位富强的君王；
这等奇事，许多人简直无从置信。

===Original===
The water-weary pilgrims,
 long-tossed upon the sea,
 When fourteen days were ended
